rather than a 16px grabber (2026-09-05) — so the first volume's head does
    // not butt against it at either step.
    const fieldPadTop = sheet === 'full' ? 60 : 62;
    const fieldPadBot = 22;
    // Sizing target is always the FULL frame, so pulling the drawer up does not
    // resize the map — half-open it simply crops.
    const fullAvail = Math.max(120, frameH - fieldPadTop - fieldPadBot);
    const base = 2 * (G.HEAD + G.ROW + trioExt) + 32;
    this.__phNeed = Math.round(base + BAND_CLOSED);
    this.__phNeedOpen = Math.round(base + BAND_OPEN);
    const fitK = this.__phNeedOpen ? Math.min(1, fullAvail / this.__phNeedOpen) : 1;
    const needNow = st.sel ? this.__phNeedOpen : this.__phNeed;
    // Measured against the CURRENT sheet's content box, and against the SCALED
    // height rather than the raw requirement.
    const fieldFits = Math.round(needNow * fitK) <= Math.max(120, sheetInnerH - fieldPadTop - fieldPadBot);
    const cellW = Math.floor((G.W - G.PAD * 2) / G.COLS);

    const connsOf = (name) => this.PH_CONNS.filter(c =>
      this.phMembersOf(c).some(m => this.slug(this.phBare(m)) === this.slug(name)));

    // ── ONE resolved answer for "is this face lit?" ─────────────────────────
    // Keyed per instance ('vol:drop:name') so the disc, its label and the cell
    // all read the same map. A name appearing twice lights ONE face — the
    // curator, unless its invitee drop is the selected one.
    const fKey = (vi, di, name) => vi + ':' + di + ':' + this.slug(name);
    const appearances = {};
    this.data('VOLS').forEach((v, vi) => v.drops.forEach((d, di) => {
      const add = (n, isC) => {
        const k = this.slug(n);
        (appearances[k] = appearances[k] || []).push({ vi: vi, di: di, isC: isC, name: n });
      };
      add(d[1], true);
      d[3].forEach(g => add(g, false));
    }));
    const litFaces = {};
    // The HELD dot wins over the hovered one, so a tap's story stays up while
    // the finger moves across neighbouring dots (2026-09-04). Same precedence
    // the desktop readout uses between selLink and hoverLink.
    const bubNow = st.bub != null ? st.bub : (st.bubHover != null ? st.bubHover : null);
    const hovered = bubNow != null ? this.PH_CONNS[bubNow] : null;
    if (hovered) {
      this.phMembersOf(hovered).forEach(raw => {
        const parts = String(raw).split(':');
        const all = appearances[this.slug(parts[0])] || [];
        if (!all.length) return;
        if (parts.length === 4) {
          // Qualified: one exact face, so both sides of a self-crossing light.
          const want = all.filter(x => x.vi === (+parts[1] - 1) && x.di === (+parts[2] - 1) && x.isC === (parts[3] === 'c'));
          if (want[0]) { litFaces[fKey(want[0].vi, want[0].di, want[0].name)] = 1; return; }
        }
        const inSel = st.sel ? all.filter(x => x.vi === st.sel.vi && x.di === st.sel.di) : [];
        const pick = inSel[0] || all.filter(x => x.isC)[0] || all[0];
        litFaces[fKey(pick.vi, pick.di, pick.name)] = 1;
      });
    }
    const anyLit = Object.keys(litFaces).length > 0;

    const bands = this.data('VOLS').map((v, vi) => ({
      key: 'b' + vi,
      // The phone map's own wording, not the site data's.
      label: 'Vol. ' + (vi + 1),
      note: (vi === 0 ? '2025' : '2026') + ' · drops 1–8',
      panelAfter: vi === 0,
      headStyle: {
        display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '10px',
        height: G.HEAD + 'px', flexShrink: 0, padding: '0 ' + G.PAD + 'px', boxSizing: 'border-box',
        fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', fontWeight: 700,
        letterSpacing: '0.16em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink70)'
      },
      noteStyle: { fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '8.5px', letterSpacing: '0.1em', color: 'var(--ink40)', flexShrink: 0 },
      gridStyle: {
        display: 'grid', gridTemplateColumns: 'repeat(' + G.COLS + ', ' + cellW + 'px)',
        padding: '0 ' + G.PAD + 'px', boxSizing: 'border-box'
      },
      drops: v.drops.map((d, di) => {
        const cName = d[1], guests = d[3];
        const on = st.sel && st.sel.vi === vi && st.sel.di === di;
        const cx = Math.round(cellW / 2), cy = G.CY;
        const gp = [{ x: cx - 22, y: cy + 31 }, { x: cx + 22, y: cy + 44 }];
        const wire = (p, lit) => {
          const dx = p.x - cx, dy = p.y - cy;
          return {
            position: 'absolute', left: cx + 'px', top: cy + 'px',
            width: Math.round(Math.sqrt(dx * dx + dy * dy)) + 'px', height: 0,
            borderTop: '1px solid ' + (lit ? 'rgba(138,106,18,0.85)' : 'rgba(23,25,27,0.16)'),
            transform: 'rotate(' + (Math.atan2(dy, dx) * 180 / Math.PI).toFixed(2) + 'deg)',
            transformOrigin: '0 0', pointerEvents: 'none', zIndex: 1,
            opacity: anyLit ? 0.12 : 1, transition: 'opacity 180ms ease'
          };
        };
        const mk = (name, isC, p, half) => {
          const size = isC ? G.C : G.I;
          const isLit = !!litFaces[fKey(vi, di, name)];
          // The label box is bounded by the CELL: an invitee sits off centre, so
          // a box centred on it would cross into the neighbouring drop.
          const bw = isC ? cellW : Math.round(cellW / 2) + 6;
          const bl = isC ? 0 : (half === 0 ? -3 : cellW - bw + 3);
          return {
            key: name + di,
            name: name,
            // §4-7 · A STABLE HANDLE ON THE FACE (2026-09-07). Engineering's only
            // handle on a curator dot was its rendered TEXT, and the corpus
            // here is artist names — which collide by nature. `handle` is this
            // file's own slug(), the same one photoFor() and every crumb use,
            // so the handle and the photograph can never name different people.
            handle: this.slug(name),
            photo: this.photoFor(name) || '',
            wrap: {
              position: 'absolute', left: bl + 'px',
              // A curator's name sits ABOVE the circle, so the box starts a name's
              // height higher and stacks in reverse — the disc still lands on p.y.
              top: (p.y - size / 2 - (isC ? G.NAME_H : 0)) + 'px',
              width: bw + 'px', display: 'flex',
              flexDirection: isC ? 'column-reverse' : 'column',
              alignItems: 'center', gap: '3px', zIndex: isLit ? 4 : 2, pointerEvents: 'none',
              opacity: anyLit ? (isLit ? 1 : 0.16) : 1,
              transition: 'opacity 180ms ease'
            },
            // The phone map's own pad (block B1). This path draws the smallest
            // dots in the file and is the one most likely to be touched.
            tapPad: {
              position: 'absolute', left: '50%', top: '50%',
              width: '44px', height: '44px', margin: '-22px 0 0 -22px',
              borderRadius: '50%', background: 'transparent', zIndex: 1
            },
            disc: {
              // pointerEvents auto: the wrap is none so a label never blocks the
              // map, but the disc must re-enable it for itself AND the badge
              // inside it — otherwise the badge renders and cannot be pressed,
              // which is the dead-control fault in another disguise.
              position: 'relative', pointerEvents: 'auto',
              left: (p.x - (bl + bw / 2)) + 'px',
              width: size + 'px', height: size + 'px', borderRadius: '50%', boxSizing: 'border-box',
              border: (isLit ? 3 : 2) + 'px solid ' + (isLit ? '#8a6a12' : (isC ? '#b4762a' : '#2f8f86')),
              background: this.photoFor(name) ? ('url(' + this.photoFor(name) + ') center/cover no-repeat, #ece9e1') : '#ece9e1'
            },
            // ══ THE PLAY BADGE, WHICH THIS SURFACE NEVER HAD (2026-09-15, Tom:
            // "we built, for desktop, a player version of the ATH landing page,
            // including the curator map with play buttons on the circles as you
            // touch them") ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
            // He is right, and I was wrong twice over: I told him this page was "a
            // hover surface with no play affordance" — asserted, never measured.
            // MEASURED: 48 discs at 390px, ZERO play marks. The desktop markup
            // renders p.monogram, p.hoverPlay, p.playClick and p.playMark; the phone
            // markup rendered only p.disc and p.tapPad, and this builder defined
            // none of the play keys. The control was never withheld by a gate — it
            // was never built here.
            //
            // PERSISTENT, NOT HOVER-REVEALED. The desktop badge appears on
            // (hov || mapSeatOn), which is the exact pattern that made the catalogue
            // grid unplayable on a phone: a control revealed by something a phone
            // does not have is a control that does not exist. Same answer as there —
            // on this surface it is simply present.
            //
            // ⚠ AND IT CARRIES THE PLAYABLE TERM, the other lesson from that round:
            // recFor(name) != null, and not pending. A badge that renders and does
            // nothing is worse than no badge — and in the tool's placeholder data
            // most of these names do not resolve, so most discs will correctly show
            // nothing here until the live PAIRS arrives.
            //
            // 26px of paint, 40px of target, by the negative-margin technique this
            // file already uses for the sheet toggles and the queue rows. On the
            // disc's lower-right perimeter rather than over the face — the desktop
            // badge's own placement, for its own reason: the photograph is what the
            // circle exists to show.
            // ⚠⚠ ON TOUCH, NOT ALWAYS (2026-09-15, Tom, with three shots of the
            // live site: "the play button shouldn't show up until you touch a
            // drop… I have no idea why you just have them on three drops and
            // not the other drops").
            //
            // Both halves of that are one mistake. I made this PERSISTENT,
            // reasoning that a phone has no hover to reveal it — the same
            // argument that was right for the catalogue grid. It is wrong here,
            // and the live site shows why: this map reveals ONE badge at a time,
            // on the face under the cursor. Nine badges at once on a 48-disc
            // deck is not a phone adaptation of that, it is a different design —
            // and because only nine names resolve a record, the nine looked
            // arbitrary, which is exactly what he saw.
            //
            // The phone's equivalent of hover is the TOUCH that lights a face:
            // isLit is litFaces[fKey(vi, di, name)], already set by tapping. So
            // the badge appears on the lit face and on whatever is sounding, and
            // nowhere else — one badge at a time, the same as the live map.
            //
            // The catalogue grid stays persistent, and that is not a
            // contradiction: a grid card has no other affordance, whereas a disc
            // here is already tappable and lighting it is the first half of the
            // gesture. Reveal-on-touch only fails when nothing reveals it.
            playBadge: ((isLit || this.mapSeatOn(st, fKey(vi, di, name), name)) && this.recFor(name) != null && !/pending/i.test(name)) ? {
              position: 'absolute', left: '85.36%', top: '85.36%',
              width: '40px', height: '40px', margin: '-20px 0 0 -20px',
              padding: '7px', boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '50%',
              backgroundClip: 'content-box',
              backgroundColor: this.mapSeatOn(st, fKey(vi, di, name), name) ? '#8a6a12' : 'rgba(255,255,255,0.94)',
              display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center',
              zIndex: 5, cursor: 'pointer', pointerEvents: 'auto'
            } : { display: 'none' },
            // The desktop's own mark, verbatim: a CSS border triangle (6×8, an exact
            // box, no font metric involved) and a two-bar pause from one element's
            // left and right borders. pointerEvents none, so a press lands on the
            // badge that owns the handler rather than on the mark inside it.
            playMark: this.mapSeatOn(st, fKey(vi, di, name), name)
              ? { width: '6px', height: '8px', boxSizing: 'border-box',
                  borderLeft: '2px solid #ffffff', borderRight: '2px solid #ffffff',
                  pointerEvents: 'none' }
              : { width: 0, height: 0,
                  borderStyle: 'solid', borderWidth: '4px 0 4px 6px',
                  borderColor: 'transparent transparent transparent #8a6a12',
                  pointerEvents: 'none' },
            // stopPropagation, or the disc's own click would select the drop at the
            // same time — a press is about audio, a tap on the disc is about the
            // composition: two intents on one dot. preventDefault too, per r47 §1.
            playClick: (e) => {
              if (e) {
                if (e.preventDefault) e.preventDefault();
                if (e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();
              }
              if (/pending/i.test(name)) return;
              const rec = this.recFor(name);
              if (rec == null) return;
              if (this.mapSeatOn(st, fKey(vi, di, name), name)) { this.setState({ paused: true }); return; }
              // ⚠ AFTER play(), not before: play() clears mapSeat — that is how a
              // press on any other surface releases the seat — so claiming it first
              // would have wiped its own claim.
              this.play(rec, 0);
              this.setState({ mapSeat: fKey(vi, di, name) });
            },
            nameStyle: {
              maxWidth: '100%', fontSize: isC ? '8px' : '7.5px',
              fontWeight: (isC || isLit) ? 600 : 400, lineHeight: 1.2, textAlign: 'center',
              color: isLit ? '#8a6a12' : 'rgba(23,25,27,0.78)',
              // Never mid-word: these are people's names.
              overflowWrap: 'normal', wordBreak: 'keep-all',
              display: '-webkit-box', WebkitBoxOrient: 'vertical', WebkitLineClamp: 2,
              overflow: 'hidden'
            }
          };
        };
        const litHere = [cName].concat(guests).some(n => litFaces[fKey(vi, di, n)]);
        return {
          key: 'd' + vi + di,
          // No magnification anywhere: selection is weight, not size.
          cellStyle: {
            position: 'relative', height: G.ROW + 'px', minWidth: 0, cursor: 'pointer',
            borderRadius: '6px',
            // NO plate behind a selected drop on the phone: the circles already
            // carry the selection (gold rings, everything else dimmed), so a
            // shaded box behind them is a second, redundant highlight.
            background: 'transparent',
            opacity: anyLit ? 1 : ((st.sel && !on) ? 0.26 : 1),
            transition: 'opacity 200ms ease'
          },
          wireA: wire(gp[0], on && !anyLit),
          wireB: wire(gp[1], on && !anyLit),
          people: [mk(cName, true, { x: cx, y: cy })]
            .concat(guests.map((g, gi) => mk(g, false, gp[gi], gi))),
          click: (e) => {
            if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();
            this.setState({ sel: (on ? null : { vi: vi, di: di }), bub: null, bubHover: null });
          }
        };
      })
    }));

    // ⚠ THE SAME CRASH SHAPE AS TITLES (2026-09-06, r44 adversarial pass).
    // `VOLS[vi].drops[di]` where st.sel was set against a LONGER VOLS than the
    // one currently injected throws inside renderVals — and a thrown
    // renderVals leaks every {{ hole }} in the template as literal text, which
    // is exactly how the design's own notes reached nnatapes.music. VOLS is
    // editorial-class, so the host may publish one volume while a held
    // selection still names volume two, and nothing validates the selection
    // against the table it indexes. So it is validated here.
    const sel = (() => {
      if (!st.sel) return null;
      const V = this.data('VOLS') || [];
      const vol = V[st.sel.vi];
      const drops = vol && vol.drops;
      return (drops && drops[st.sel.di]) || null;
    })();
    const selNames = sel ? [sel[1]].concat(sel[3]) : [];
    const withConns = selNames.filter(n => connsOf(n).length);
    const bandOpen = !!(sel && withConns.length);

    const PASTEL = ['#d8a06a', '#8fc7b6', '#8fa8cf', '#e0b07e', '#8fc4cf', '#c9a2bd'];

    return {
      clearAll: () => this.setState({ sel: null, bub: null }),

      // The card behind the drawer: its own header, so the drawer reads as a
      // layer over a page rather than the page itself.
      // ── THE HEAD IS AS TALL AS ITS TEXT (2026-09-06, Tom) ─────────────────
      // It was a fixed 210px box and the drawer started at 210, so the dead
      // band was INSIDE the head — below the last line, above the drawer. A
      // fixed height for text that wraps differently per device could only be
      // right at one width anyway.
      // Auto height now, and the drawer starts where the head actually ends
      // (headRef measures it). The gap between them is one value, GAP below.
      cardHeadStyle: sheet === 'full' ? { display: 'none' } : {
        position: 'absolute', left: 0, right: 0, top: 0, zIndex: 1,
        padding: '26px 20px 0', boxSizing: 'border-box',
        display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', gap: '10px'
      },
      // Measured rather than assumed, the same rule the card centring settled
      // on: reasoning about a box in two coordinate spaces is what kept that
      // one open for three reports. Only writes when the rounded value moves,
      // so it cannot loop.
      headRef: (el) => {
        if (!el) return;
        const h = Math.round(el.getBoundingClientRect().height / (this._stageK(el) || 1));
        if (h > 40 && h !== this.state.mapHeadH) this.setState({ mapHeadH: h });
      },
      sheetOpen: sheet === 'full',
      toggleSheet: (e) => {
        if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();
        this.setState({ sheet: sheet === 'full' ? 'half' : 'full', sel: null, bub: null });
      },
      // The drawer itself. Half-open it sits below the card's header; pulled up
      // it covers the card entirely.
      drawerStyle: {
        // ⚠ THE SHEET STOPS AT THE BAR (2026-09-11) ─────────────────────────
        // It ran to bottom:0 with the transport painted over it, so on every
        // phone except 390×844 the constellation block — label, names and
        // ⤢ Open — was drawn UNDERNEATH the bar and could not be scrolled to:
        // the content fitted the scroller, so there was nothing to scroll.
        //
        // A bottom PADDING cannot fix that. Padding extends a scroll area; it
        // does not move content up, and with scrollHeight === clientHeight
        // there was no scrolling for it to extend. The sheet has to not be
        // there — so its box ends where the bar begins, and every child is
        // inside the visible region by construction rather than by arithmetic.
        //
        // 88 = the bar's 12px inset + its 76px height, the same one
        // measurement heroLockStyle and flatPaneStyle already clear.
        //
        // ⚠⚠ THIS drawerStyle DOES NOT STYLE THE RENDERED DRAWER. There are
        // TWO keys of this name — this one, returned from phoneMap(), and the
        // live one in renderVals() (search "drawerStyle: narrow ?"). The live
        // one wins, and it is where the transport reserve belongs.
        //
        // I got this wrong twice in one turn and both failures are instructive.
        // First I wrote `(narrow && barShown)` HERE: those are renderVals
        // consts ~4,300 lines below and nothing in this method can see them,
        // so the page died on "narrow is not defined". Then I "fixed" the
        // crash by hard-coding a literal into this copy — which rendered
        // nothing at all, because this copy does not render. The scope error
        // was real; the key was always the wrong one.
        //
        // Left at 0 so the file does not carry two contradictory statements of
        // one geometry. If this copy ever becomes live, the reserve has to
        // come with it.
        position: 'absolute', left: 0, right: 0, bottom: 0,
        // Half-open, the drawer starts where the head ENDS plus one gap —
        // 210px was a guess that left a dead band under the text (2026-09-06).
        // 14 is the gap; the 210 fallback only applies for the single frame
        // before the head has been measured.
        top: sheet === 'full' ? '0px' : ((st.mapHeadH ? st.mapHeadH + 14 : 210) + 'px'),
        // Above the site header when it owns the screen.
        zIndex: sheet === 'full' ? 12 : 4, boxSizing: 'border-box',
        background: 'var(--bg)',
        borderTop: '1px solid var(--hair20)',
        borderRadius: sheet === 'full' ? 0 : '16px 16px 0 0',
        boxShadow: sheet === 'full' ? 'none' : '0 -16px 40px rgba(23,25,27,0.14)',
        overflow: 'hidden',
        transition: 'top 380ms cubic-bezier(.4,0,.2,1), border-radius 380ms ease'
      },
      // ── STOP ADVERTISING A PULL (2026-09-05, Tom) ─────────────────────────
      // The drawer said "Pull up the drawer to explore" and a pull did not open
      // it — it flipped to the next card. That is not a bug in the drawer; it
      // is TWO THINGS CLAIMING ONE AXIS. Vertical on this surface belongs to
      // the deck (card → card), which is the landing's primary gesture, so
      // making the pull work would mean taking card navigation away. The
      // drawer cannot win that argument and should not be entered into it.
      //
      // So the drawer stops pretending. The grabber line — a mark that means
      // "drag me" in every app a visitor has ever used — becomes a LABELLED
      // BUTTON that says what it does, and the button is the whole width so it
      // cannot be mistaken for something to drag. Tap to open, tap to close,
      // stated both times.
      //
      // The label is the destination, not the mechanic: "Open the map" rather
      // than "Tap to open", because a visitor wants the map and does not need
      // to be taught the gesture for a button.
      grabStyle: {
        position: 'absolute', left: 0, right: 0, top: 0, height: '48px', zIndex: 6,
        display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center', gap: '8px',
        boxSizing: 'border-box', cursor: 'pointer',
        borderBottom: '1px solid var(--hair)',
        background: 'var(--bg)',
        fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9.5px',
        letterSpacing: '0.16em', textTransform: 'uppercase',
        color: '#8a6a12'
      },
      // The old grabber, kept as a hidden constant rather than deleted: it is
      // referenced by the two OTHER phone sheets (the label menu and the record
      // sheet), which ARE pull-and-tap because nothing competes for the axis
      // there. Only this drawer's is a button.
      grabBarStyle: { display: 'none' },
      grabLabel: sheet === 'full' ? 'Close the map' : 'Open the map',
      grabGlyph: sheet === 'full' ? '\u2715' : '\u2191',
      grabGlyphStyle: { fontSize: '11px', lineHeight: 1, color: '#8a6a12' },
      drawerHintStyle: { display: 'none' },
      drawerHint: '',
      // The card is still a CARD: the label and arrow at the foot are how you
      // flip to the next one, so the fade has to clear them properly. It starts
      // midway through Volume Two's first row and is fully opaque well before
      // the nav, instead of the old 56px sliver that left the text sitting on
      // top of faces.
      drawerFadeStyle: sheet === 'full' ? { display: 'none' } : {
        position: 'absolute', left: 0, right: 0, bottom: 0,
        height: Math.round(sheetInnerH * 0.46) + 'px', zIndex: 5,
        background: st.theme === 'light'
          ? 'linear-gradient(rgba(255,255,255,0) 0%, rgba(255,255,255,0.72) 46%, #ffffff 74%, #ffffff 100%)'
          : 'linear-gradient(rgba(6,7,10,0) 0%, rgba(6,7,10,0.72) 46%, #06070a 74%, #06070a 100%)',
        pointerEvents: 'none'
      },
      // Half-open, the sheet is one big "open me" — but NOT the foot of the card.
      // The bottom strip belongs to the card's own next-panel control, so the tap
      // layer stops short of it and the arrow stays free.
      mapOpenTapStyle: sheet === 'full' ? { display: 'none' } : {
        position: 'absolute', left: 0, right: 0, top: '16px', bottom: '96px', zIndex: 6,
        cursor: 'pointer', background: 'transparent'
      },

      // Requirement, derived: two volumes of (head + one row of pitch + the last
      // row's own trio extent), plus the connections band at its OPEN height,
      // plus the field's padding.
      scalerStyle: (() => {
        const need = this.__phNeedOpen || 0;
        // ONE scale factor, computed above against the field's real content box.
        const k = fitK;
        return {
          display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', flexShrink: 0,
          height: (need ? Math.round(needNow * k) : 0) + 'px',
          margin: 'auto 0',
          transform: k < 1 ? 'scale(' + k.toFixed(4) + ')' : 'none',
          transformOrigin: 'top center'
        };
      })(),
      fieldStyle: {
        position: 'absolute', left: 0, right: 0, top: 0, bottom: 0,
        // The 16px grab handle sits on the top edge, so half-open the field
        // needs real clearance under it — at 16px the first volume's head was
        // butting straight against the drawer button.
        padding: fieldPadTop + 'px 0 ' + fieldPadBot + 'px', boxSizing: 'border-box',
        // Centred only when the composition FITS. Half-open it does not, and
        // centring overflow crops equally off both ends — which is what pushed
        // Volume One's head up out of view. Then it flows from the top.
        display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column',
        justifyContent: fieldFits ? 'center' : 'flex-start',
        // Inert until the drawer is up: half-open the map is a picture of
        // itself, and a tap anywhere opens it rather than selecting a drop.
        pointerEvents: sheet === 'full' ? 'auto' : 'none',
        overflowY: (sheet === 'full' && !st.sel && this.__phNeedOpen > frameH - 10) ? 'hidden' : 'auto',
        overflowX: 'hidden', scrollbarWidth: 'none'
      },

      bands: bands.map((b2, i) => Object.assign({}, b2, {
        headStyle: Object.assign({}, b2.headStyle, i === 0 ? { marginTop: 'auto' } : {}),
        gridStyle: Object.assign({}, b2.gridStyle, i === bands.length - 1 ? { marginBottom: 'auto' } : {})
      })),

      // ══ RESTORED VERBATIM FROM THE 2026-09-14 18:00 FILE (2026-09-15, Tom) ══
      // "Make sure, as a starting place, the mobile curator map matches what we
      // had, and you're not just making this up from scratch."
      //
      // He is right and this is a revert, not a redesign. What I replaced it
      // with was absolutely-positioned children inside a box that had gone back
      // to growing — so the story text and the dot rows, which the flow column
      // below keeps apart with gap: 8px, were laid on top of each other. The
      // overlap he reported is entirely mine.
      //
      // ⚠ THE SHAKE IS NOT FIXED BY THIS AND IS NOT FIXED AT ALL. This is the
      // behaviour of yesterday's file, shake included. Do not treat the revert
      // as a fix. What is now known, and is worth more than another guess:
      //   · bandOpen is driven by sel — a CLICK. The shake happens on HOVER,
      //     where the only thing that changes is the story TEXT. So the box
      //     opening is not the loop; the story is the only moving part.
      //   · storyStyle has a FIXED height (STORY_H) and flexShrink: 0, so its
      //     own box does not resize with the text — which means the next
      //     measurement should be whether the text WRAPS to a different number
      //     of lines inside it, and whether the clump columns below reflow.
      //   · Measure the hovered dot's own getBoundingClientRect across the
      //     hover, and count enter/leave pairs per second. Do not change a
      //     number in here before one of those two readings exists.

      // The middle band: a thin spacer closed, the clumps when a drop is open.
      bandStyle: {
        marginTop: bandOpen ? '-14px' : 0,
        marginBottom: 0,
        height: (bandOpen ? BAND_OPEN : 10) + 'px',
        padding: bandOpen ? '0 ' + G.PAD + 'px 0' : '0 ' + G.PAD + 'px',
        boxSizing: 'border-box', overflow: 'hidden', flexShrink: 0,
        display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', justifyContent: 'flex-start', gap: '8px',
        paddingTop: bandOpen ? '12px' : 0,
        transition: 'height 300ms cubic-bezier(.4,0,.2,1), padding 300ms ease'
      },
      bandRowStyle: { flex: 1, minHeight: 0, display: 'flex', alignItems: 'flex-start', justifyContent: 'center', gap: '8px' },
      bandTitleStyle: bandOpen
        ? {
            display: 'block', textAlign: 'center',
            fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '8px', fontWeight: 700,
            letterSpacing: '0.2em', textTransform: 'uppercase',
            color: 'var(--ink40)'
          }
        : { display: 'none' },
      // The story sits directly above the dots, where the eye already is.
      story: hovered ? this.phNoteOf(hovered) : '',
      storyStyle: !bandOpen
        ? { display: 'none' }
        : {
            // Reserved height so the columns below never shift, but sized to the
            // TEXT rather than to a guess: at 30px this box was cutting the
            // third line off every longer connection note.
            height: STORY_H + 'px', flexShrink: 0,
            display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center',
            padding: '0 6px', boxSizing: 'border-box',
            textAlign: 'center', fontSize: '9.5px', fontStyle: 'italic',
            lineHeight: 1.4, color: '#8a6a12', textWrap: 'pretty'
          },

      // An artist with no connections gets no column.
      clumps: (() => {
        const rowsFor = (w) => {
          const c = connsOf(w).length;
          const cw = Math.floor((G.W - G.PAD * 2 - (withConns.length - 1) * 8) / Math.max(1, withConns.length));
          const perRank = Math.max(1, Math.floor(((cw - 6) + 4) / (11 + 4)));
          return Math.min(2, Math.ceil(c / perRank));
        };
        const bandRanks = withConns.reduce((m, w) => Math.max(m, rowsFor(w)), 1);
        return withConns.map((who, gi) => {
        const mine = this.PH_CONNS.map((c, ci) => ({ c: c, ci: ci }))
          .filter(x => this.phMembersOf(x.c).some(m => this.slug(this.phBare(m)) === this.slug(who)));
        const cols = withConns.length;
        const GAPX = 8;
        const colW = Math.floor((G.W - G.PAD * 2 - (cols - 1) * GAPX) / cols);
        const room = colW / 2 - 3;
        // A 7px mono label fits roughly 15 characters per line in this column.
        const nameLines = who.length > Math.floor((colW - 6) / 4.3) ? 2 : 1;
        const L = this.phLayoutClump(mine.map(x => x.ci), room, room, nameLines, bandRanks);
        return {
          key: 'cl' + gi,
          name: who,
          colStyle: { flex: 1, minWidth: 0, display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', alignItems: 'center', gap: '7px' },
          // Centred at the top of the clump box, with a known width so the arc
          // can be told where it may not go.
          nameStyle: {
            position: 'absolute', left: '50%', top: 0, transform: 'translateX(-50%)',
            width: '100%', maxWidth: '100%', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '7px',
            letterSpacing: '0.01em', textTransform: 'uppercase', textAlign: 'center',
            lineHeight: 1.25,
            color: 'var(--ink55)', whiteSpace: 'normal', overflowWrap: 'normal',
            zIndex: 3, pointerEvents: 'none'
          },
          clumpStyle: { position: 'relative', width: '100%', height: L.boxH + 'px', overflow: 'visible' },
          // ── THE CONNECTION DOTS ARE TAPPABLE, AND THEY HOLD (2026-09-04) ──
          // Two things made these mouse-only:
          //
          //   1. ONE STATE FOR TWO GESTURES. `bub` was set by mouseenter and
          //      CLEARED BY MOUSELEAVE. A tap fires a synthetic mouseenter and
          //      then a mouseleave the instant the finger moves or anything
          //      else is touched — so the bubble opened and vanished. Hover and
          //      hold are different intents and now have different state:
          //      `bubHover` is the mouse's (transient, cleared on leave) and
          //      `bub` is the HELD one, set by click and cleared only by
          //      another click or the map's own clear. Either shows the bubble.
          //   2. THE TARGETS ARE 5–11px. That is the drawn size of a dot in a
          //      clump and it should stay that size — this is a density
          //      diagram. So a transparent 44px pad is centred on each, the
          //      same pattern as the curator dots.
          //
          // Click SETS rather than toggles: "holds the tooltip until I click
          // another" is the ask, and a toggle means a second tap on a 7px dot
          // closes what you were reading. Clearing is the map's own job —
          // tapping empty space already calls clearSel.
          dots: mine.slice(0, L.pts.length).map((x, i) => {
            const P = L.pts[i], d = P.d;
            // ── THE PAD IS CLAMPED TO THE DOT PITCH (2026-09-15, Tom) ────────
            // "The first dot on the left does not shake. The second one does."
            // Measured, pixel by pixel, across Alan Sparhawk's four dots: every
            // dot's hover region sat ONE DOT TO THE LEFT of the dot you can
            // see. Visible spans 352–360, 363–372, 376–385, 388–398; ownership
            // seams at 352, 365, 377. A 44px pad on a ~17px pitch overlaps both
            // neighbours, and later siblings paint on top, so each pad covered
            // the dot to its left.
            //
            // That is both faults in one geometry: the FIRST dot is entirely
            // inside its neighbour's pad, so it reads steadily — and reads the
            // WRONG connection; the second has the seam 2px inside its circle,
            // so a resting pointer alternates between two dots and the note and
            // ring swap with it. Not one dot toggling: two dots trading the
            // pointer, which is why it read as "flashing between the connected
            // curator pictures".
            //
            // Width only. The pads tile at exactly the pitch, so every seam
            // falls midway BETWEEN two dots instead of inside one, and each dot
            // owns the circle you can see. Height stays 44 — vertically there
            // is nothing to collide with, so the finger keeps its full reach on
            // the axis that has room. Never narrower than the drawn dot.
            //
            // ⚠ THIS IS NOT THE 09-09 COUNTER-SCALE AND DOES NOT REPLACE IT.
            // That one keeps the pad 44px while the dot grows; this one stops
            // the pads overlapping in the first place. Both are needed: the
            // counter-scale was in place the whole time this was shaking.
            const pitch = (() => {
              const xs = L.pts.map(p => p.cx);
              let m = Infinity;
              if (i > 0) m = Math.min(m, Math.abs(xs[i] - xs[i - 1]));
              if (i < xs.length - 1) m = Math.min(m, Math.abs(xs[i + 1] - xs[i]));
              return isFinite(m) ? m : 44;
            })();
            const padW = Math.max(d, Math.min(44, pitch));
            const on = st.bub === x.ci || st.bubHover === x.ci;
            // A single row, staggered a few px so it reads as a clump rather
            // than a ruler — no second rank, so the clump is one dot tall.
            return {
              key: 'dt' + x.ci,
              title: this.phMembersOf(x.c).map(m => this.phBare(m)).join(' ↔ '),
              style: {
                position: 'absolute',
                left: 'calc(50% + ' + P.cx.toFixed(1) + 'px - ' + (d / 2) + 'px)',
                top: P.y + 'px',
                width: d + 'px', height: d + 'px', borderRadius: '50%',
                background: PASTEL[(x.ci + gi) % PASTEL.length],
                // overflow visible by default, so the 44px pad centred inside
                // this 7px dot extends past it and is still hittable.
                boxShadow: on ? '0 0 0 3px rgba(138,106,18,0.34)' : '0 0 0 1px rgba(23,25,27,0.12)',
                transform: on ? 'scale(1.45)' : 'none',
                cursor: 'pointer',
                transition: 'transform 160ms ease, box-shadow 160ms ease'
              },
              // The 44px finger target: centred on the drawn dot, transparent,
              // so the diagram's density is untouched while the thing you can
              // hit is a finger wide. It carries the click; the dot beneath
              // keeps the visual state.
              padStyle: {
                position: 'absolute', left: '50%', top: '50%',
                width: padW + 'px', height: '44px',
                margin: '-22px 0 0 ' + (-padW / 2) + 'px',
                // ⚠ THE PAD MUST NOT GROW WITH THE DOT (2026-09-09, Tom: in the
                // sidebar map "the text shakes… and so does the dot"). The pad
                // is a CHILD of the dot, so the hover scale multiplied it too:
                // 44px became 64px the instant the dot lit, and at the column's
                // width the pads of neighbouring dots already overlap. A still
                // cursor could therefore end up over a different pad than the
                // one it entered — enter, leave, enter — which reads as a shake
                // in both the dot and the story the hover writes. Counter-
                // scaling keeps the hit area exactly 44px in both states, so the
                // target under the cursor cannot change without the cursor
                // moving. The drawn dot still grows; only the invisible target
                // holds still.
                transform: on ? 'scale(' + (1 / 1.45).toFixed(4) + ')' : 'none',
                borderRadius: '50%', background: 'transparent', cursor: 'pointer', zIndex: 2
              },
              enter: () => this.hoverEnter({ bubHover: x.ci }),
              leave: () => this.setState({ bubHover: null }),
              // Sets, never toggles — see the note above.
              click: (e) => { if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation(); this.setState({ bub: x.ci, bubHover: null }); }
            };
          })
        };
        });
      })(),

      // The floor names the drop and nothing else — connections live above.
      floorStyle: {
        display: 'none',
        position: 'absolute', left: 0, right: 0, bottom: 0, height: G.READER + 'px', zIndex: 6,
        boxSizing: 'border-box', padding: '12px 16px',
        background: 'var(--bg)', borderTop: '1px solid var(--hair20)'
      },
      floorKicker: sel ? ('Vol. ' + (st.sel.vi + 1) + ' · Drop ' + sel[0]) : 'Across the Horizon · the curator map',
      floorKickerStyle: { fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '8.5px', letterSpacing: '0.2em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: '#8a6a12' },
      // The totals were the words "Sixteen" and "forty-eight". Counted from
      // VOLS now, so a new drop changes this line (2026-09-04).
      floorTitle: sel ? sel[1] : (() => {
        const vols = this.data('VOLS') || [];
        let drops = 0, people = 0;
        vols.forEach(v => (v.drops || []).forEach(d => { drops++; people += 1 + (d[3] || []).length; }));
        return drops ? (drops + ' drops, ' + people + ' artists') : 'The series';
      })(),
      floorTitleStyle: { marginTop: '7px', fontSize: '16px', fontWeight: 600, letterSpacing: '-0.015em', lineHeight: 1.2 },
      floorSub: sel
        ? (sel[2] + '  ·  with ' + sel[3].join(' and '))
        : 'Each curator brings two artists in. Tap any drop to open its connections, then a dot to read one.',
      floorSubStyle: { marginTop: '5px', fontSize: '11.5px', lineHeight: 1.5, color: 'var(--ink70)' }
    };
  }

  // ── §1 · PIN THE LABEL BEFORE THE ROUTE MOVES (2026-09-07) ──────────────
  // labelKeyOf() falls back to the route prefix when no host published a key —
  // which is what keeps this file openable on its own. But that fallback is
  // exactly what breaks the moment an in-page navigation leaves an `ath-`
  // route: the prefix stops matching and the key would fall to Northern Spy,
  // reproducing the very bug §1 fixes whenever the file is opened without a
  // host (the design tool, every review, every screenshot).
  //
  // So the key is FROZEN at the first navigation, from the state that is still
  // correct, and carried from then on. In production it is already set at
  // arrival and this is a no-op. Every navigator calls it — go(), goAth(),
  // goRoute(), goCard() and the deep-link openers — so there is one place a
  // future route can be added without having to know about any of this.
  // ── §1 · BELT AND BRACES, NOT THE MECHANISM (2026-09-07) ────────────────
  // ⚠ CORRECTED. This comment previously claimed "every navigator calls it —
  // go(), goAth(), goRoute(), goCard() and the deep-link openers — so there is
  // one place a future route can be added". That was FALSE and it mattered:
  // eight other setters change the route without coming through here —
  // openAlbum(), the four browse/filter commits, the ⋮ menu's artistPage and
  // artistMap, and the ath-track artist link — several reachable from ATH as a
  // visitor's first action. An out-of-date authority is worse than none (see
  // the ledger's own rule), and a load-bearing claim that is wrong about its
  // own coverage is the worst kind.
  //
  // So the mechanism is NOT here. labelKey is resolved non-null at arrival (see
  // the block at the end of arrival()), which means setState's merge carries it
  // through every setter whether it knows about this or not. This method only
  // handles the case where a key is somehow still absent, and gives the dev
  // picker its one legitimate way to CHANGE the label rather than carry it.
  pinLabel(patch, force) {
    // `force` is for the callers allowed to change the label rather than carry
    // it — the dev picker (the tool's site switcher) and goAth, which states ATH.
    if (force) return Object.assign({ labelKey: force }, patch);
    const st = this.state;
    return st && st.labelKey ? patch : Object.assign({ labelKey: this.labelKeyOf(st) }, patch);
  }
  goRoute(r, asLabel) {
    const FLAT = { tour: 1, news: 1, about: 1, record: 1, radio: 1, catalogue: 1 };
    this.setState(this.pinLabel({
      route: r, menuOpen: false, browseOpen: false, explore: false, cart: false, queue: false, hoverStar: null,
      // The group is DARK on every way in (Tom, 2026-09-03) — the picker's
      // 'r:group' lands here, not in goGroup. Other routes leave the theme alone.
      theme: r === 'group' ? 'dark' : (r === 'cls' || r.indexOf('cls-') === 0) ? 'light' : this.state.theme,
      // A flat route owns the screen; leaving the column on 'tour' would light
      // the drawer's rail stub at the same time as the page.
      view: FLAT[r] ? 'releases' : this.state.view
    }, asLabel));
  }

  // The flat album PAGE (route 'record') — the whole-window album, as opposed
  // to openAlbum below, which opens the same record inside the star map's
  // column. A cover in the catalogue grid wants this one.
  openRecord(rec) {
    this.setState(this.pinLabel({ route: 'record', recIdx: rec, lock: rec, prev: 'catalogue', open: true, heroMenu: false, browseOpen: false, explore: false, cart: false, queue: false }));
  }

  openAlbum(rec, fromView) {
    // ⚠ DOOR 2 OF 4, and the one that would otherwise have broken the phone
    // outright: this is how EVERY record opens — a grid cover, a hero, a queue
    // row — and it set route 'star' unconditionally, so with no map a phone
    // could not open a record at all.
    //
    // openRecord() is the answer and it already existed: route 'record', the
    // flat whole-window album page. Its own comment calls it "the whole-window
    // album, as opposed to openAlbum below, which opens the same record inside
    // the star map's column… a cover in the catalogue grid wants this one."
    // With no map on a phone, EVERY cover is a cover in the grid.
    if (this.phoneNoMap()) return this.openRecord(rec);
    // Always lands in the star view: the record lives in that column, so
    // opening one from the landing or a flat page has to switch worlds too.
    this.setState({ route: 'star', view: 'album', lock: rec, prev: fromView || this.state.prev || 'releases', hoverStar: null, open: true, heroMenu: false, browseOpen: false, explore: false, cart: false });
  }

  // The artists index mosaic. Every src is a real file in this project and every
  // ar is its MEASURED ratio: jeremy-cunningham.jpg is 2400×1600 (1.5), the
  // twelve public/ath/photos are 600² (1.0). Two entries carry no photo and say
  // so — the ratios are declared so the wall proves landscape, portrait and
  // square, and reads real ratios untouched when the originals sync.
  // [name, src, ar, albums, focus]
  // [name, src, ar, albums, hometown, focus] — hometowns are 3a's own values;
  // everything else says "hometown pending" rather than inventing a place.
  /* ══ DC-DEFAULTS:START run=10 drop=INDEX,PHOTOS ══ */
  DEFAULT_INDEX = [
    ['Jeremy Cunningham', 'public/artists/jeremy-cunningham.jpg', 1.5, 2, 'Chicago, Illinois', '18%'],
    ['Balmorhea', 'public/ath/photos/balmorhea.jpg', 1.0, 1, 'Austin, Texas'],
    ['Rachel Grimes', 'public/ath/photos/rachel-grimes.jpg', 1.0, 1, ''],
    ['Takeshi Nishimoto', 'public/ath/photos/takeshi-nishimoto.jpg', 1.0, 1, ''],
    ['Clarice Jensen', 'public/ath/photos/clarice-jensen.jpg', 1.0, 1, ''],
    ['marine eyes', 'public/ath/photos/marine-eyes.jpg', 1.0, 1, ''],
    ['Alan Sparhawk', 'public/ath/photos/alan-sparhawk.jpg', 1.0, 1, ''],
    ['Mary Lattimore', 'public/ath/photos/mary-lattimore.jpg', 1.0, 1, ''],
    ['Chuck Johnson', 'public/ath/photos/chuck-johnson.jpg', 1.0, 1, ''],
    ['Carlos Niño', 'public/ath/photos/carlos-nino.jpg', 1.0, 1, ''],
    ['East Forest', 'public/ath/photos/east-forest.jpg', 1.0, 1, ''],
    ['Liz Draper', 'public/ath/photos/liz-draper.jpg', 1.0, 1, ''],
    ['Kenneth Gregory', 'public/ath/photos/kenneth-gregory.jpg', 1.0, 1, ''],
    ['The Necks', '', 1.5, 1, ''],
    ['Adjuster66', '', 0.667, 1, '']
  ];

  DEFAULT_PHOTOS = {
    'Jeremy Cunningham': [
      ['public/artists/jeremy-cunningham.jpg', 1.5, 'press 1 · 2400×1600', '18%'],
      ['', 0.667, 'portrait slot'],
