      ['', 1.778, 'live shot slot']
    ],
    'Balmorhea': [['public/ath/photos/balmorhea.jpg', 1.0, 'press 1 · 600²'], ['', 1.5, 'landscape slot'], ['', 0.667, 'portrait slot']],
    'Clarice Jensen': [['public/ath/photos/clarice-jensen.jpg', 1.0, 'press 1 · 600²'], ['', 1.5, 'landscape slot'], ['', 0.667, 'portrait slot']],
    'Rachel Grimes': [['public/ath/photos/rachel-grimes.jpg', 1.0, 'press 1 · 600²'], ['', 1.778, 'live shot slot'], ['', 0.75, 'portrait slot']],
    'marine eyes': [['public/ath/photos/marine-eyes.jpg', 1.0, 'press 1 · 600²'], ['', 1.5, 'landscape slot']]
  };

  /* ══ DC-DEFAULTS:END run=10 drop=INDEX,PHOTOS ══ */
  // Hero stack per label. Northern Spy's three are real files measured in this
  // project: jeremy-cunningham 2400×1600 (landscape), stephanie-coleman 597×397
  // (landscape), dave-harrington 928×1400 (PORTRAIT) — so the orientation rule
  // is visible rather than asserted. Nobody's photo is captioned with someone
  // else's name; where we hold no release title it says so.
  /* ══ DC-DEFAULTS:START run=11 keep=HEROES ══ */
  DEFAULT_HEROES = {
    0: [
      { kicker: 'Out now · NS-118', title: 'Passing Fires', artist: 'Jeremy Cunningham', src: 'public/artists/jeremy-cunningham.jpg', ar: 1.5, rec: 0 },
      { kicker: 'Out now', title: 'Release title pending', artist: 'Stephanie Coleman', src: 'public/artists/stephanie-coleman.jpg', ar: 1.504, pending: true },
      { kicker: 'Out now', title: 'Release title pending', artist: 'Dave Harrington', src: 'public/artists/dave-harrington.jpg', ar: 0.663, pending: true }
    ]
  };

  /* ══ DC-DEFAULTS:END run=11 keep=HEROES ══ */
  heroSet(label) {
    const live = this.data('HEROES')[label];
    // ── THE HERO IS CACHED, SO A RETURN VISIT NEVER WAITS (2026-09-05, Tom:
    // "it only changes once a month… no excuse for it being slow to load or
    // having a skeleton") ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    // Right, and the frequency is the argument: a value that changes monthly
    // has no business being fetched before the page can be drawn. r32 removed
    // the specimen copy, which fixed the lie but left the emptiness — and an
    // empty hero is only the honest answer on a FIRST visit.
    //
    // So the last known set is kept per label and painted immediately, and the
    // live table replaces it when it arrives. Cached-then-corrected is the
    // right shape for content this stable; a skeleton is what you show when you
    // have nothing, and after one visit we have something.
    //
    // Per label, because each has its own three. Written on every live read so
    // the cache follows the label's real picks without a separate invalidation
    // step — the month it changes, the first visitor after the change writes
    // the new one and everybody else gets it instantly.
    if (live && live.length) {
      this._heroCacheWrite(label, live);
      return live;
    }
    const cached = this._heroCacheRead(label);
    if (cached && cached.length) return cached;
    // First visit with nothing fetched: the photograph's ground and no words,
    // per r32. Never specimen copy.
    return [{ kicker: '', title: '', artist: '', src: '', ar: 1.5, pending: true }];
  }
  // localStorage, and it only ever writes keys it owns (sa.hero.<label>). A
  // read that throws — private mode, a quota, a hostile value — returns null
  // and the caller falls through to the empty frame, so the cache can fail
  // without taking the page with it.
  _heroCacheKey(label) { return 'sa.hero.' + (label == null ? 0 : label); }
  _heroCacheRead(label) {
    try {
      if (typeof localStorage === 'undefined') return null;
      const raw = localStorage.getItem(this._heroCacheKey(label));
      if (!raw) return null;
      const v = JSON.parse(raw);
      // Shape-checked rather than trusted: a stale key from an older release
      // must not be able to render a hero with no title.
      if (!Array.isArray(v) || !v.length) return null;
      return v.filter(h => h && typeof h.title === 'string' && h.title);
    } catch (e) { return null; }
  }
  _heroCacheWrite(label, set) {
    try {
      if (typeof localStorage === 'undefined') return;
      const key = this._heroCacheKey(label);
      // Only the fields the hero paints, so a big injected row cannot bloat
      // the cache, and only when something actually changed.
      //
      // ⚠ AND NEVER A PENDING ROW (2026-09-06). `title` is truthy for
      // 'Release title pending', so this stored it happily — and a cached
      // placeholder outlives the table that produced it, which is worse than
      // the placeholder itself. Only rows with real copy are worth remembering.
      const lean = set.filter(h => h && !h.pending && h.title).map(h => ({
        kicker: h.kicker || '', title: h.title || '', artist: h.artist || '',
        // ar: null, NOT 1.5 (2026-09-09, code audit part 3). Rule 2 names this
        // exact spelling — "ar: 1 was the same bug" — and this one was WRITTEN TO
        // localStorage, so a fabricated landscape ratio outlived the table it came
        // from and the same row painted differently from cache than from live.
        // focus RIDES WITH THE ROW (2026-09-10). heroSet paints from this
        // cache on a return visit, so a field the cache drops is a field that
        // silently stops working for everyone who has been here before — the
        // hero would crop correctly on a first visit and revert afterwards.
        // null, not '', for the same reason as ar: absent must stay absent so
        // heroPhoto's fallback fires rather than reading an empty position.
        src: h.src || '', ar: h.ar == null ? null : h.ar, rec: h.rec == null ? null : h.rec,
        focus: h.focus == null ? null : h.focus
      }));
      if (!lean.length) return;
      const next = JSON.stringify(lean);
      if (this['_heroCached' + label] === next) return;
      this['_heroCached' + label] = next;
      localStorage.setItem(key, next);
    } catch (e) { /* a cache that cannot write is still a working page */ }
  }

  toHero(i, h) {
    if (this._scroller) this._scroller.scrollTo({ top: i * h, behavior: 'smooth' });
  }

  photos(name) {
    const set = this.data('PHOTOS')[name] || [['', 1.5, 'landscape slot'], ['', 0.667, 'portrait slot'], ['', 1.0, 'square slot']];
    return set.map(p => ({ src: p[0], ar: p[1], label: p[2], focus: p[3] }));
  }
  // ── ONE PORTRAIT SOURCE FOR EVERY PAGE (2026-09-04) ───────────────────────
  // Any surface that shows a face reads it HERE, keyed by the artist's name,
  // and never by a path typed at the call site. Order:
  //   1. PHOTOS[name]  — the ranked press set, alternates and all
  //   2. INDEX[name][1] — the roster's single measured portrait
  //   3. ''            — no photo: the design's hatch, never someone else's face
  portraitsFor(name) {
    const set = this.photos(name).filter(p => p.src);
    if (set.length) return set;
    const row = this.data('INDEX').filter(x => x[0] === name)[0];
    return (row && row[1]) ? [{ src: row[1], ar: row[2] || 1, label: 'roster portrait', focus: row[5] }] : [];
  }
  // ── ONE TOUR SOURCE, KEYED BY ARTIST (2026-09-04) ─────────────────────────
  // Every "On tour" block on every page reads this. It returns rows in ONE
  // shape — [date, venue, city, badge, ticketUrl] — for THAT artist and no
  // one else. Nothing on the site may render an unfiltered tour table: a
  // record page showing another artist's dates is worse than showing none.
  //
  // Source order, first that yields rows wins:
  //   1. TOUR            [artist, date, venue, city, badge, ticketUrl] — the
  //                      table to inject. Artist-keyed, month-agnostic, the
  //                      only one that scales past two months.
  //   2. TOUR_APRIL / TOUR_MAY   [day, dow, artist, 'Venue · City', note,
  //                      photo] — the label tour page's own tables, filtered
  //                      to this artist; the month comes from the table name.
  //   3. REC_TOUR_EVENTS [date, venue, city, badge, artist?] — accepted ONLY
  //                      where a row names this artist in a 5th element. The
  //                      4-column form carries no artist, so it can only be
  //                      the label tour page's own list and is NOT used here.
  // No rows is a real answer: the caller hides its block.
  tourFor(name) {
    if (!name) return [];
    const T = this.data('TOUR') || [];
    // TOUR[6] is the artist's press photo (2026-09-05). Carried through as the
    // 6th element so any surface reading tourFor has it; the tour page's own
    // grouping reads TOUR directly and is handled there.
    const keyed = T.filter(r => r[0] === name).map(r => [r[1], r[2], r[3], r[4] || '', r[5] || '', r[6] || '']);
    if (keyed.length) return keyed;
    const month = (rows, m) => (rows || []).filter(r => r[2] === name).map(r => {
      const where = String(r[3] || '').split(' \u00b7 ');
      return [m + ' ' + r[0], where[0] || '', where[1] || '', r[4] || '', ''];
    });
    // ── CATALOGUE DATA OUTRANKS EDITORIAL COPY (2026-09-04, ledger 19) ─────
    // TOUR_APRIL / TOUR_MAY are editorial-class, so in site mode data() falls
    // through to this file's literals — and those literals name REAL catalogue
    // artists at real venues on dates that do not exist. An artist we hold no
    // dates for was printing an invented one. REC_TOUR_EVENTS (catalogue) is
    // consulted BEFORE the month tables now, and the months are listed in
    // SITE_EMPTY_WHEN_ABSENT so site mode empties them as their own comment
    // always promised.
    const keyed2 = (this.data('REC_TOUR_EVENTS') || [])
      .filter(e => e[4] && e[4] === name)
      .map(e => [e[0], e[1], e[2], e[3] || '', e[5] || '']);
    if (keyed2.length) return keyed2;
    return month(this.data('TOUR_APRIL'), 'Apr').concat(month(this.data('TOUR_MAY'), 'May'));
  }
  // ── ONE VIDEO SOURCE, KEYED BY ARTIST AND OPTIONALLY BY RECORD ────────────
  // (2026-09-04.) Every video block on every page reads this. VIDEOS rows are
  // [artist, title, url, thumb, recordTitle?]:
  //   · filtered to the artist, always;
  //   · narrowed further to ONE record when a record title is passed AND any
  //     of that artist's rows carry a 5th element — so a record page shows
  //     the videos for THAT record, and an artist with untagged rows still
  //     shows their channel rather than nothing.
  // url is the real destination (a YouTube watch url); thumb is the poster
  // frame. Neither is ever invented here — no rows means no videos on file,
  // and the caller says so. The three striped boxes this replaced were a
  // placeholder the site had no way to fill.
  videosFor(name, recordTitle) {
    if (!name) return [];
    const mine = (this.data('VIDEOS') || []).filter(v => v[0] === name);
    if (!recordTitle) return mine;
    const tagged = mine.filter(v => v[4]);
    if (!tagged.length) return mine;
    return tagged.filter(v => v[4] === recordTitle);
  }
  // The head a video block wears, from the count alone: the codebase's rule —
  // "Video" for one, a count for many.
  // ── THE VIDEO ID, FROM WHATEVER THE ROW CARRIES (2026-09-05) ─────────────
  // VIDEOS rows hold a watch url and a thumbnail; the embed needs the bare id.
  // Both carry it, so neither has to change shape: watch?v=ID, youtu.be/ID,
  // /embed/ID, or the thumbnail's own /vi/ID/ path.
  ytId(row) {
    const url = String((row && row[2]) || '');
    let m = url.match(/[?&]v=([\w-]{6,})/) || url.match(/youtu\.be\/([\w-]{6,})/) || url.match(/\/embed\/([\w-]{6,})/);
    if (m) return m[1];
    m = String((row && row[3]) || '').match(/\/vi\/([\w-]{6,})\//);
    return m ? m[1] : '';
  }
  // ── THE EMBED PLAYER, AS THE LIVE SITE BUILDS IT (2026-09-05, Tom) ───────
  // Transcribed from components/ui/LiteYouTube.tsx + VideoCarousel.tsx rather
  // than invented, because the drop was showing anchor tiles that sent people
  // to youtube.com — off the site, which is the opposite of the intent.
  //
  // The live pattern, exactly:
  //   · ONE 16:9 player box at the top. Until pressed it is a poster frame
  //     with the YouTube glyph over it; pressed, it becomes an iframe on
  //     youtube-nocookie.com/embed/ID?autoplay=1&rel=0 — IN PLACE. Nothing
  //     opens a new page.
  //   · A thumbnail strip below it, only when there is more than one video,
  //     with an "N / total" counter. Picking a thumb changes the active video.
  //   · The poster is hqdefault.jpg, NOT maxresdefault — LiteYouTube's own
  //     comment records that maxres only exists for uploads ≥720p, so about a
  //     tenth of older videos 404. Do not "upgrade" this.
  //   · #0a0e1a behind, a 0.28-alpha cream hairline, and the glyph in #e8b04a:
  //     the live component's own colours, which are not this file's tokens
  //     because the player is a YouTube surface and reads as one.
  //
  // key scopes the open/active state so two blocks on one page keep their own.
  videoPanel(rows, key) {
    const list = (rows || []).map(r => ({ id: this.ytId(r), title: r[1] || '', thumb: r[3] || '' })).filter(v => v.id);
    const st = this.state;
    const idx = Math.min(Math.max(0, (st.ytIdx || {})[key] || 0), Math.max(0, list.length - 1));
    const active = list[idx] || null;
    const open = !!active && (st.ytOpen || {})[key] === active.id;
    const poster = (v) => v.thumb || ('https://i.ytimg.com/vi/' + v.id + '/hqdefault.jpg');
    return {
      any: list.length > 0,
      count: list.length,
      title: active ? active.title : '',
      // The iframe's src is empty until the poster is pressed, so no YouTube
      // request is made — and no cookie set — for a video nobody asked to see.
      // ⚠ about:blank, not '' (2026-09-06). An <iframe src=""> re-requests the
      // CURRENT DOCUMENT in every browser — so an unopened player quietly
      // fetched the page again, three times per record page. Adjacent to the
      // four 404s Claude Code measured, and the same rule: a src attribute is
      // never harmlessly empty.
      embedSrc: open ? ('https://www.youtube-nocookie.com/embed/' + active.id + '?autoplay=1&rel=0') : 'about:blank',
      boxStyle: list.length
        ? { position: 'relative', width: '100%', aspectRatio: '16 / 9', overflow: 'hidden', background: '#0a0e1a', border: '1px solid rgba(244,237,224,0.28)', borderRadius: '6px' }
        : { display: 'none' },
      frameStyle: open
        ? { width: '100%', height: '100%', border: 'none', display: 'block' }
        : { display: 'none' },
      posterStyle: (!open && active)
        ? { position: 'absolute', inset: 0, width: '100%', height: '100%', border: 'none', padding: 0, cursor: 'pointer', background: 'url(' + poster(active) + ') center/cover no-repeat, #0a0e1a' }
        : { display: 'none' },
      // ── V2 · THE VIDEO POSTER'S ▶︎ GOES TOO (2026-09-07, Tom) ──────────────
      // I argued this one should stay: it is a YouTube control, it opens the
      // embed, it never touches the transport or the queue, and hover audio
      // cannot play it. Tom: "you need to get rid of all play buttons in hover
      // mode. All play buttons."
      //
      // He is right and my reasoning was the wrong kind of correct. The rule is
      // about what the visitor SEES, not about which subsystem a control talks
      // to: a ▶︎ in hover mode teaches that this site has play buttons, and one
      // surviving glyph undoes the lesson the mode exists to give. "All" is
      // also a rule that can be checked; "all except the ones that are really
      // video" is a judgement call every future audit has to re-litigate.
      //
      // The poster still opens on click — the tile is the control, exactly as a
      // cover is in hover mode. It just no longer advertises itself with a
      // glyph.
      glyphStyle: this.playCtl((!open && active)
        ? { position: 'absolute', inset: 0, display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center', opacity: 0.85, fontSize: '34px', color: '#e8b04a', pointerEvents: 'none' }
        : { display: 'none' }),
      capStyle: (!open && active && active.title)
        ? { position: 'absolute', left: 0, right: 0, bottom: 0, padding: '20px 10px 8px', fontSize: '11.5px', lineHeight: 1.3, color: '#f3f1ea', background: 'linear-gradient(180deg, rgba(0,0,0,0) 0%, rgba(0,0,0,0.72) 100%)', pointerEvents: 'none' }
        : { display: 'none' },
      openPlayer: (e) => {
        if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();
        if (!active) return;
        this.setState({ ytOpen: Object.assign({}, this.state.ytOpen, { [key]: active.id }) });
      },
      // The counter and strip appear only past one video, as in VideoCarousel.
      counter: list.length > 1 ? ((idx + 1) + ' / ' + list.length) : '',
      counterStyle: list.length > 1
        ? { marginTop: '8px', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.18em', color: 'var(--ink40)', textAlign: 'right', fontVariantNumeric: 'tabular-nums' }
        : { display: 'none' },
      stripStyle: list.length > 1
        ? { marginTop: '6px', display: 'flex', gap: '6px', overflowX: 'auto', paddingBottom: '2px' }
        : { display: 'none' },
      thumbs: list.map((v, i) => ({
        key: 'yt' + key + i,
        title: v.title,
        style: {
          position: 'relative', flexShrink: 0, width: '104px', aspectRatio: '16 / 9',
          borderRadius: '4px', boxSizing: 'border-box', cursor: 'pointer',
          border: '1px solid ' + (i === idx ? 'var(--gold)' : 'rgba(244,237,224,0.28)'),
          background: 'url(' + poster(v) + ') center/cover no-repeat, #0a0e1a',
          opacity: i === idx ? 1 : 0.72
        },
        // Picking a thumb makes it active AND opens it, because a press on a
        // video is a request to watch that video — VideoCarousel's own
        // behaviour, where the reload key remounts the player on every pick.
        pick: (e) => {
          if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();
          this.setState({
            ytIdx: Object.assign({}, this.state.ytIdx, { [key]: i }),
            ytOpen: Object.assign({}, this.state.ytOpen, { [key]: v.id })
          });
        }
      }))
    };
  }
  // Binds openConstellation to one anchor artist. A method rather than three
  // inline closures, so the surfaces cannot drift apart again (2026-09-05).
  rv_openCon(who) {
    return (e) => {
      if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();
      const isStarNow = this.state.route === 'star';
      this.setState({
        route: isStarNow ? 'star' : 'catalogue',
        view: 'results',
        constellation: true,
        conAnchor: who || '',
        resultCat: 'Constellation',
        resultLabel: who || '',
        open: true, browseOpen: false, explore: false, cart: false, queue: false,
        // ⚠ THE HELD RECORD HAS TO BE RELEASED (2026-09-05). I first kept the
        // lock in star mode, reasoning that the map should keep its held star.
        // But the drawer's album panel is gated on that lock, so keeping it
        // meant the panel stayed up and the constellation's list never
        // appeared — the control looked like it did nothing on the very
        // surface it matters most. Opening a SET means the set is what the
        // column shows; `constellation: true` keeps the field lit, which is
        // what holding the constellation actually looks like.
        lock: null, starLock: null, hoverStar: null
      });
    };
  }
  // The See-our-work jump, as a method so the header control and the phone
  // drawer call the same one (2026-09-05).
  clsSeeWorkNow() {
    const pane = document.querySelector('[data-surface="clandestine"]');
    const el = pane && pane.querySelector('[data-cls-work]');
    if (!pane || !el) return;
    // offsetTop, summed up to the pane — NOT getBoundingClientRect. The
    // artboard is scaled in tool mode, so client rects are in screen px while
    // scrollTop is in layout px, and the two disagree by the scale factor: a
    // rect-based jump landed ~1300px short at 924 wide. Offsets are
    // layout-space and correct in both modes.
    let top = 0;
    for (let n = el; n && n !== pane; n = n.offsetParent) top += n.offsetTop;
    pane.scrollTop = Math.max(0, top - 24);
  }
  // The hero's advance, shared by the wheel, the pager dots and the touch
  // handler (2026-09-05). Clamped to the set, with the same 420ms cooldown the
  // wheel had — a swipe emits a burst of events and without it a single flick
  // would skip the middle hero.
  heroGo(i) {
    const now = Date.now();
    if (this._lastFlip && now - this._lastFlip < 420) return;
    const all = this.heroSet(this.state.label);
    const next = Math.min(all.length - 1, Math.max(0, i));
    if (next === (this.state.hero || 0)) return;
    this._lastFlip = now;
    this.setState({ hero: next, hhover: null });
  }
  videoLabel(n, scope) {
    // Zero does not get a count. The rule is "Video" for one and a count for
    // many; "Videos · 0 for this record" over an empty-state note said the same
    // thing twice and read like a failed fetch.
    if (!n) return 'Video';
    return n === 1 ? 'Video' : ('Videos \u00b7 ' + n + ' ' + (scope || ''));
  }
  // The tracklist rows THE PAGE RENDERS — one source, so the list and the line
  // under it can never disagree. Site: REC_TRACKS narrowed to this record.
  // Tool: the mock, whose first title is the record's own.
  recTrackRows(pair) {
    const t1 = pair ? pair[1] : 'Track pending';
    const key = this.recKey(pair);
    const isSite = !!(this.props.site || (typeof window !== 'undefined' && window.__DC_SITE));
    return isSite
      ? this.tracksFor(key)
      // READ THE INJECTED KEYS IN BOTH BRANCHES (2026-09-04, designer request
      // 11). This branch reached for the DEFAULT_ constants directly, so a host
      // injection of REC_TRACKS_FIRST_DUR / REC_TRACKS_REST was silently
      // discarded in the tool. data() falls back to the same literals anyway.
      : [[t1, this.data('REC_TRACKS_FIRST_DUR')]].concat(this.data('REC_TRACKS_REST') || []);
  }
  // ── ONE TRACKLIST SOURCE, KEYED BY RECORD (2026-09-04) ──────────────────
  // ⚠ THE STALE HALF OF THIS NOTE IS DELETED (2026-09-09, code audit part 1). It
  // said rows are [name, duration, recordTitle?] and are narrowed by the record's
  // TITLE — the join ledger 20 removed. The row shape is below and the join is
  // recKey(pair); whoever read the wrong one joined on the wrong string.
  //
  // Where the rows carry the 3rd element they are narrowed to ONE record, which
  // is what stops a catalogue of 489 records showing the same nine tracks on
  // every page. Untagged rows are taken as this record's, the shape the drop
  // shipped with. Track one is the record's own title by construction.
  // REC_TRACKS rows are [title, duration, recordKey?, artist?]. The 4th element
  // is the TRACK's own credit — needed on splits and compilations, where the
  // record's artist is not the track's (r5 ask 6). Absent → the record's.
  tracksFor(key) {
    const rows = this.data('REC_TRACKS') || [];
    const tagged = rows.filter(t => t[2]);
    if (tagged.length && key) return tagged.filter(t => t[2] === key);
    return rows;
  }
  // ── VOLS → ATH_DROPS, ONE JOIN (2026-09-04, ledger 15 + 18) ──────────────
  // VOLS carries the drop's SHAPE — curator, guests, one track title. ATH_DROPS
  // carries the fuller record: every track's own credit, title, label and
  // cover. The volumes list and the landing's Latest Drop were built from VOLS
  // alone, so two of every three tracks read "Title pending" and every cover
  // tile drew the hatch — while the rail's own cards, one function away, read
  // ATH_DROPS correctly. The join is by title: 'Vol. N Drop N' matches
  // ATH_DROPS[].title.
  //   tracks[ci] = [credit, title, label, portrait, cover]
  athDropRow(title) {
    return (this.data('ATH_DROPS') || []).filter(d => d.title === title)[0] || null;
  }
  // ── A DROP'S TRACKS RESOLVE HERE, NOT IN THE TABLE (2026-09-07, r48) ─────
  // DEFAULT_ATH_DROPS used to carry `tracks: this.data('ATH_TRACKS_A')` inside
  // the literal, which was wrong twice.
  //
  //   1. A CLASS FIELD INITIALISER CANNOT BE READ AS DATA. Engineering's
  //      installer evaluates each DEFAULT_ literal on its own to record what a
  //      drop ships with, and there `this` is unbound — so the table read as
  //      "could not evaluate" rather than as rows or as empty. 45 of 46 read;
  //      this was the one. A DEFAULT_ table is a value, and now it is one.
  //   2. IT RESOLVED ONCE, AT CONSTRUCTION. Field initialisers run while the
  //      component is being built, before the host's tables are readable, so
  //      the row captured the DEFAULT tracks and kept them for the life of the
  //      component. An injected ATH_TRACKS_A would never have reached the
  //      volumes list, the landing's Latest Drop or the ath-track page — the
  //      exact class of fault as "read the injected keys in BOTH branches"
  //      (2026-09-04, designer request 11), one scope further out.
  //
  // So the literal names its table (`tracksTable`) and this resolves it at read
  // time. A host row that carries `tracks` inline still wins, so injecting the
  // fuller shape needs no change on their side.
  athDropTracks(row) {
    if (!row) return [];
    if (row.tracks) return row.tracks;
    return row.tracksTable ? (this.data(row.tracksTable) || []) : [];
  }
  // Track ci of that drop, or null. Absent is absent: the caller keeps its
  // pending note and its hatch rather than borrowing a neighbour's.
  athDropTrack(title, ci) {
    const t = this.athDropTracks(this.athDropRow(title))[ci];
    return t || null;
  }
  // THE RECORD'S KEY (2026-09-04, ledger 20). REC_TRACKS / REC_NOTES /
  // REC_COVERS / REC_CONTRIBUTORS were joined on pair[1], the DISPLAYED title —
  // and four live records collapse to "S/T", two to "Split", so one record's
  // tracklist and liner note reached another's page. PAIRS[6] is the record id
  // when the site supplies one; the title stays the fallback so nothing breaks
  // before it does.
  recKey(pair) {
    if (!pair) return '';
    return pair[6] || pair[1] || '';
  }
  // ── NO SECTION WITHOUT DATA (2026-09-04, Tom: "We don't print sections that
  // don't have data. There's no reason to do that.") ─────────────────────────
  // A block whose data is absent does not render — no heading, no rule, no
  // empty-state sentence. The earlier convention (a note reading "No videos on
  // file.") was still furniture: it spent a row of the page telling a visitor
  // about a gap that is our problem, not theirs.
  //
  // The gap is not lost, though — it is REPORTED. Every absence passes through
  // missing() and lands in window.__DC_MISSING, so the build can harvest the
  // list and go find the data instead of discovering it by eye:
  //
  //   window.__DC_MISSING        → [{ key, scope, subject }, …]
  //   window.__DC_MISSING_REPORT() → a grouped console table
  //
  // Idempotent: renderVals runs on every paint, so entries are de-duped by
  // key|scope|subject. It records only what a surface ACTUALLY ASKED FOR and
  // did not get, which is the difference between this list and a schema.
  missing(key, scope, subject) {
    if (typeof window === 'undefined') return false;
    // NO SUBJECT, NO GAP. On the star view with nothing locked, the record
    // surface still evaluates — pair is null — so four record-scope absences
    // were recorded for no record at all, and they were the first rows the
    // build would harvest. An absence is only an absence when something asked
    // for a named subject.
    if (!subject) return false;
    const id = key + '|' + (scope || '') + '|' + (subject || '');
    this._miss = this._miss || {};
    if (!this._miss[id]) {
      this._miss[id] = true;
      window.__DC_MISSING = window.__DC_MISSING || [];
      window.__DC_MISSING.push({ key: key, scope: scope || '', subject: subject || '' });
      if (!window.__DC_MISSING_REPORT) {
        window.__DC_MISSING_REPORT = () => {
          const by = {};
          (window.__DC_MISSING || []).forEach(m => { (by[m.key] = by[m.key] || []).push(m.scope + (m.subject ? (' \u00b7 ' + m.subject) : '')); });
          Object.keys(by).sort().forEach(k => console.log('[drop] missing ' + k + ' \u00d7' + by[k].length, by[k]));
          return window.__DC_MISSING;
        };
      }
    }
    return false;
  }
  // ── A ROW RESOLVES LIKE THE PAGE DOES (2026-09-04) ───────────────────────
  // The page-level control resolves the drop's curator and sounds; the row
  // level resolved the ROW's artist and often did not, because an invited
  // artist frequently has no record under their own name — recFor() returned
  // null and the click fell through to play() with no argument, which resumes
  // whatever the transport held. Same resolver, one more hop: the row's own
  // artist first, then the drop's curator, whose record IS the drop. The track
  // index still selects the row within it, so the right thing sounds either
  // way. This is the one place both tiers agree on identity.
  dropRowRec(r, name) {
    const mine = this.recFor(name);
    if (mine != null) return mine;
    const viaDrop = r && r.curator ? this.recFor(r.curator) : null;
    return viaDrop != null ? viaDrop : null;
  }
  // Is THIS row of THIS drop the one sounding? Four things have to agree: the
  // transport is running and not paused, the record matches, the row index
  // matches, and the drop the transport was started from matches. Without the
  // last one an artist on two drops lit a row in each (2026-09-04).
  dropRowOn(st, r, name, ti) {
    if (!st.started || st.paused) return false;
    const rec = this.dropRowRec(r, name);
    // ⚠ AN UNNAMED SUBJECT STILL SOUNDS (2026-09-05). Where neither the row's
    // artist nor the drop's curator resolves to a record, the press went
    // through play() with no argument and the transport ran anyway — so
    // returning false here left the control saying "Play" over audible sound,
    // the same defect as the ATH page header (r16). The drop the press came
    // from is the identity in that case: dropPlay was set by the press.
    if (rec == null) return st.dropPlay === ('d' + r.vi + '-' + r.di);
    if (st.npPlay !== rec) return false;
    if ((st.trackIdx == null ? 0 : st.trackIdx) !== ti) return false;
    return !st.dropPlay || st.dropPlay === ('d' + r.vi + '-' + r.di);
  }
  // ── IS THIS ROW'S COVER UNDER THE CURSOR? (2026-09-07, Tom) ───────────────
  // The hover half of dropRowOn, declared beside it so the two cannot drift.
  // st.dropHover is 'd<vi>-<di>-<ci>' and is set by the cover's own enter/leave
  // — the same key the quote overlay reads — so the row and the cover are lit by
  // one fact and cannot disagree about which track that is.
  //
  // ⚠ IT IS GATED ON hoverArmed. A cover under the cursor with hover audio OFF
  // previews nothing, so lighting the row and turning its glyph to ❙❙ would be a
  // control reporting sound that is not happening — the r16 defect in reverse.
  // Off, the cover still dims and shows its quote; the row stays quiet, which is
  // the truth. (On ATH this is armed by default, so on the surface Tom is
  // describing it is always live.)
  dropRowHov(st, r, ti) {
    if (!st || st.dropHover == null) return false;
    if (!this.hoverArmed(st)) return false;
    return st.dropHover === ('d' + r.vi + '-' + r.di + '-' + ti);
  }
  // One bar of the transport's level meter. LEVEL[i] is 0..1; absent, or not
  // playing → the 3px floor, unanimated, which reads as silence rather than as
  // a broken meter (r5 ask 4).
  // ── THE METER IS OUT FOR LAUNCH (2026-09-05, Tom) ────────────────────────
  // "Take the meter out for launch; if the audio engine grows band magnitudes
  // later it can return." Every bar is display:none and NOTHING else moves —
  // the transport's layout, the drawer row's, and the LEVEL reader all stay as
  // they are, so restoring it is deleting this one early return.
  // Not deleted outright, because the reason it existed is still true: when a
  // bar IS drawn it must read a real signal and never a timer. @keyframes
  // sa-vu is already gone (r5), and leaving the machinery intact is what stops
  // an animated fake being re-added in its place.
  // ── THE METER IS LIVE AGAIN (2026-09-06, Tom) ────────────────────────
  // ⚠ THIS REVERSES r21, WHICH WAS TOM'S OWN INSTRUCTION — "take the meter out
  // for launch; if the audio engine grows band magnitudes later it can return."
  // It is back at his request: "I'm also no longer seeing any audio bars in
  // this transport… they need to be put back. The transport needs to be alive."
  // Recorded rather than silently flipped, so nobody reads r21's note and
  // removes it again.
  //
  // The r21 machinery was deliberately left intact, so this is the deletion of
  // one early return. Everything r5 established still holds and is the reason
  // the meter can be trusted: the bars read LEVEL, the host's live 0..1 band
  // magnitudes, and NEVER a timer. @keyframes sa-vu is still gone.
  //
  // WITH NO SIGNAL THE BARS SIT AT THEIR FLOOR rather than hiding — a quiet
  // meter beside a playing track says "the engine is not publishing levels",
  // which is a true and useful thing to see. Hiding them made a missing signal
  // look identical to a missing feature, which is how this went unnoticed.
  // ── THE SPEC FOR LEVEL, IN ONE PLACE (2026-09-06, Tom: "did you give all the
  // information Claude Code needs?") ────────────────────────────────────────
  // Everything the host needs, so nobody has to infer it from a bar's height:
  //
  //   LEVEL = [n, n, n, n, n]        five numbers, 0..1, published on the
  //                                  transport callback like NOW_PLAYING
  //
  //   · FIVE values. The transport draws five bars; the drawer row draws the
  //     FIRST FOUR of the same array. Publishing five covers both — there is
  //     no separate four-band signal to compute.
  //   · 0..1 per band, low to high. Clamped here, so an out-of-range value is
  //     survivable rather than fatal.
  //   · Publish on the same cadence as position (~10–20/s is plenty; the bars
  //     carry a 90ms height transition, so anything faster is invisible).
  //   · [] or absent → the bars sit at their 3px floor in hairline grey. That
  //     is the designed state for "the transport is running and the engine is
  //     not sending levels", and it is NOT the same as hiding them.
  //   · NEVER synthesised here. The r5 rule stands: a bar that is drawn reads
  //     a real signal or it reads nothing. @keyframes sa-vu is deleted.
  //
  // Two places consume it, and both were live before r21 removed them:
  //   the transport bar (five bars, beside the scrubber)
  //   the star drawer's release rows (four bars, marking the sounding row)
  //
  // Proven consuming correctly on 2026-09-06 with a star locked and playing:
  // no signal → heights 3 3 3 3 3 grey; LEVEL [0.2,0.85,0.5,1,0.35] →
  // 5 12 9 14 7 all gold; signal removed → 3 3 3 3 3 again.
  vuBar(i) {
    const lv = this.data('LEVEL') || [];
    // Hover preview counts as sounding — see _sounding(). A meter that ignores
    // the hover is the same defect as the play glyph that ignored it.
    const live = this._sounding();
    const v = (live && typeof lv[i] === 'number') ? Math.max(0, Math.min(1, lv[i])) : 0;
    return {
      width: '2px', height: (3 + Math.round(v * 11)) + 'px', borderRadius: '2px',
      background: v > 0 ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--hair20)',
      transition: 'height 90ms linear, background 200ms linear'
    };
  }
  // The switch's own meter. Same reader, same rules, shorter bars: 11px against
  // the transport's 14, because this is chrome on a page rather than the player
  // itself. Declared beside vuBar deliberately — two meters, ONE signal, so a
  // change to what counts as sounding reaches both in one edit.
  vuBarSmall(i) {
    const lv = this.data('LEVEL') || [];
    const live = this._sounding();
    const v = (live && typeof lv[i] === 'number') ? Math.max(0, Math.min(1, lv[i])) : 0;
    return {
      width: '2px', height: (3 + Math.round(v * 8)) + 'px', borderRadius: '2px',
      background: v > 0 ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--hair20)',
      transition: 'height 90ms linear, background 200ms linear'
    };
  }
  // ── THE HERO'S EQUALISER HAS THE HERO'S LADDER (2026-09-10) ──────────────
  // Tom: "on hover I see Listening, but I don't see an audio visual situation,
  // so I'm not sure what it looks like." Two reasons, both real:
  //
  //   1. vuBarSmall paints its floor in var(--hair20) — the THEME's hair. The
  //      hero lockup is over a photograph and carries its own ladder in
  //      literals (#f3f1ea, #DBBF3D) for exactly this reason, so a themed
  //      colour there resolves against the wrong ground and disappears. The
  //      bars were painting; they were painting in an invisible colour.
  //   2. LEVEL is not published in the design tool, so every bar sits at its
  //      3px floor. That IS correct — a meter reads a real signal or it reads
  //      nothing (r5 ask 4, and @keyframes sa-vu stays deleted) — but a 2px
  //      wide, 3px tall floor in the wrong colour is indistinguishable from an
  //      empty button, which is why the design could not be judged.
  //
  // So this is vuBarSmall in the hero's own colours, with a floor you can SEE:
  // 3px wide, 4px of 13 at rest at 45% gold, full gold and full height on
  // signal. Nothing is synthesised — the height still comes only from LEVEL,
  // so on the live site these move and here they show their shape.
  // ══ THE METER GOES WHERE THE MOUSE MAKES THE SOUND (2026-09-10, Tom) ═════
  // "Add the audio visualisers to the places where the mouseovers happen — in
  // grid mode the covers need one ON the cover, and in tracklists it needs to
  // be next to the track name, so you can see right where your mouse is. We
  // don't need the visualiser in the header any more."
  //
  // That is the better idea and it replaces mine. A meter in the header says
  // "audio is running somewhere"; a meter under the cursor says "YOU are doing
  // this", which is the whole lesson of hover mode. The header slot is deleted
  // rather than kept as a second opinion.
  //
  // ⚠ ONE BUILDER FOR ALL FOUR SURFACES — covers, album tracklists, the
  // column's tracklist and the hero button. Same LEVEL, same ladder, so no two
  // meters on one page can disagree about the level (rule 3). The gold ladder
  // is literal rather than themed because a cover is a PHOTOGRAPH: var(--*)
  // resolves against the page and vanished on the hero for two rounds.
  //
  // ⚠ NOTHING IS SYNTHESISED. Height comes only from LEVEL, so with none
  // published the bars show their shape and hold still (r5 ask 4; @keyframes
  // sa-vu stays deleted).
  // `lo`/`hi` are the ladder's floor and ceiling in px, defaulting to the
  // 3→11 the rows and the hero have always used. The COVER plate passes a
  // taller pair (2026-09-10) — see coverVuStyle — because a square plate that
  // holds an 11px ladder is mostly empty plate. Still ONE builder and one
  // signal: only the ladder's scale is a caller's business, never the value.
  hoverVu(i, lo, hi) {
    const lv = this.data('LEVEL') || [];
    const live = this._sounding();
    const v = (live && typeof lv[i] === 'number') ? Math.max(0, Math.min(1, lv[i])) : 0;
    const f = lo == null ? 3 : lo, t = hi == null ? 11 : hi;
    return {
      width: '3px', height: (f + Math.round(v * (t - f))) + 'px', borderRadius: '2px', flexShrink: 0,
      background: v > 0 ? '#DBBF3D' : 'rgba(219,191,61,0.45)',
      transition: 'height 90ms linear, background 200ms linear'
    };
  }
  // The row/cover meter's box. `on` is whether THIS surface is the one under
  // the cursor — every caller answers it from its own hover fact, so a meter
  // cannot appear on a row the mouse is not on.
  // ── THE HOVER METER BELONGS TO HOVER MODE (2026-09-14, Tom, with a phone
  // screenshot: "you have the mouseover audio visualizer and the play button
  // conflicting, but now it's just player mode for mobile, so it shouldn't
  // have the other one. They never should be conflicting anyway because
  // they're different modes.") ─────────────────────────────────────────────
  //
  // He is right on both halves, and the second is the deeper one.
  //
  // WHY IT APPEARED ON A PHONE AT ALL. The three callers gate on a HOVER
  // TARGET (st.catHover === i, colHovOn, hovOn), never on the MODE. Mobile
  // browsers dispatch mouseover/mouseenter on a tap for anything that looks
  // interactive, so tapping a cover set catHover and the meter drew itself —
  // even though hoverArmed() has returned false on a phone since the map came
  // out, and touchHoverBridge() is inert. A gate on the target is not a gate
  // on the mode.
  //
  // WHY IT WAS A BUG ON DESKTOP TOO. playCtl() is `playMode ? style : none` —
  // the play control knows which mode it belongs to. This did not, so in
  // PLAYER mode, hovering a cover drew a level meter for a preview that mode
  // does not have. One line makes this the exact mirror of playCtl, and the
  // two controls can then never be live together.
  //
  // ⚠ THAT MATTERS BECAUSE THEY SHARE ONE CORNER. The catalogue card puts both
  // at left:8 bottom:8 of the cover — deliberately, since each is the same
  // thing in its own mode, and both belong where the eye already is. The
  // overlap in his screenshot is what identical coordinates look like when
  // both render; with the modes exclusive it is now impossible by
  // construction rather than avoided by arithmetic.
  // ⚠ st IS REQUIRED, AND THAT IS THE POINT (2026-09-14, second pass).
  // The first version of this gate read this.hoverArmed(THIS.STATE) while every
  // sibling key in the same render read the `st` renderVals was handed. Those
  // are the same object on an ordinary render and NOT the same on a probe pass
  // — this file renders against synthetic viewports (PROBE_VPS, the fit passes)
  // where `narrow` differs from the live state. So the meter could answer for
  // the real viewport while the markup around it was built for the probe one:
  // one render holding two different truths, which is the shape of bug that
  // produces no error and no crash.
  //
  // Taking st as the FIRST argument makes it impossible to call this without
  // saying which state you mean. The gate still lives in one place — a control
  // that appears in one mode and not the other must never be decided locally,
  // the same rule playCtl states — but it can no longer disagree with its own
  // render.
  //
  // ⚠ playCtl(style) HAS THE IDENTICAL EXPOSURE and is deliberately NOT changed
  // in this pass: it reads this.playMode(this.state) across 26 call sites, and
  // rewriting all 26 at the end of a long session is the riskier move. It is
  // recorded in the handover as the one known instance of this pattern left.
  hoverVuBox(st, on, extra) {
    if (!on || !this.hoverArmed(st)) return { display: 'none' };
    return Object.assign({
      display: 'flex', alignItems: 'flex-end', gap: '2px', height: '11px', flexShrink: 0
    }, extra || {});
  }
  hoverVuBars(n, lo, hi) {
    const out = [];
    for (let i = 0; i < (n || 5); i++) out.push({ key: 'hv' + i, style: this.hoverVu(i, lo, hi) });
    return out;
  }

  heroVuBar(i) {
    const lv = this.data('LEVEL') || [];
    const live = this._sounding();
    const v = (live && typeof lv[i] === 'number') ? Math.max(0, Math.min(1, lv[i])) : 0;
    return {
      width: '3px', height: (3 + Math.round(v * 8)) + 'px', borderRadius: '2px', flexShrink: 0,
      background: v > 0 ? '#DBBF3D' : 'rgba(219,191,61,0.45)',
      transition: 'height 90ms linear, background 200ms linear'
    };
  }

  // ── IS SOMETHING SOUNDING RIGHT NOW? (2026-09-06) ─────────────────────
  // One answer for every control that reflects playback, because the transport
  // had been reading `started && !paused` — which hover audio never sets. So on
  // a hover preview the cover appeared and the progress bar lit, and the button
  // still said Play: two parts of one bar disagreeing about whether audio was
  // running. "The transport needs to be alive" is exactly that.
  //
  // Sounding = the transport is running, OR a hover preview is under the
  // cursor. The second is what makes moving across the star field or the grid
  // feel like playback rather than like a picture of it.
  _sounding() {
    const st = this.state;
    if (st.started && !st.paused) return true;
    const t = this._playTuple(st);
    return !!(t && t.hoverAudio && t.hoverRec != null);
  }
  vuBarRetired(i) {
    const lv = this.data('LEVEL') || [];
    const playing = !!this.state.started && !this.state.paused;
    const v = (playing && typeof lv[i] === 'number') ? Math.max(0, Math.min(1, lv[i])) : 0;
    return {
      width: '2px', height: (3 + Math.round(v * 11)) + 'px', borderRadius: '2px',
      background: v > 0 ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--hair20)',
      transition: 'height 90ms linear, background 200ms linear'
    };
  }
  // Seconds from an 'm:ss' / 'h:mm:ss' duration string; 0 when unparseable, so
  // a missing duration costs the total nothing rather than printing NaN.
  secs(d) {
    const p = String(d || '').split(':').map(n => parseInt(n, 10));
    if (p.some(isNaN) || !p.length) return 0;
    return p.reduce((a, n) => a * 60 + n, 0);
  }
  // "9 tracks · 54 min", composed from the rows on the page. It was typed, so
  // it said nine and fifty-four minutes over any tracklist of any length.
  trackFoot(rows) {
    const n = rows.length;
    if (!n) return '';
    const total = rows.reduce((a, t) => a + this.secs(t[1]), 0);
    const mins = Math.round(total / 60);
    return n + (n === 1 ? ' track' : ' tracks') + (mins ? (' \u00b7 ' + mins + ' min') : '');
  }
  // ── THE COVER SET FOR ONE RECORD (2026-09-04) ───────────────────────
  // PAIRS[rec][3] is the front cover and [4] the first alternate; any further
  // shots come from REC_COVERS[title] (an array of paths — back, insert, label,
  // whatever the release has). The "1 / N" badge and the thumbnail strip both
  // count THIS array, so a record with one cover says 1 / 1 and shows one
  // thumbnail instead of two empty hatched slots.
  coversFor(pair) {
    if (!pair) return [];
    const extra = (this.data('REC_COVERS') || {})[this.recKey(pair)] || [];
    return [pair[3], pair[4]].concat(extra).filter(Boolean);
  }

  // ── Context menus ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  // ONE design for every ⋮ in the product. Rules: it is an overlay, anchored to
  // the control that opened it and floating above the page (it never pushes
  // layout — that was the album column shoving its own title up); it opens
  // UPWARD when there is room, so the row you clicked stays visible; a fixed
  // 18px icon column; 40px rows; sections and hairline rules to group; a ▸ tail
  // for anything that leads somewhere else, ↗ for anything that leaves the site.
  // Icons are the design's own glyph set, not a second icon language.
  // [icon, label, tail, action]  ·  '--' = rule  ·  '§ Name' = section
  CTX = {
    // ── THE MENU BELONGS TO THE EPISODE, NOT THE STRAND (2026-09-05, Tom) ──
    // Each broadcast strand carried a ⋮ beside its Play control — three of
    // them, all inert (they had a style and no handler at all, so they were
    // decoration). And a strand-level menu could not have worked anyway: a
    // strand is a SHOW, and everything anyone wants from this surface is about
    // one EPISODE — queue that one, play that one next, link to that one.
    // So the three go and every episode row gets its own.
    //
    // Queueing is the reason Tom asked, and it is first. The play rows come
    // next because the row itself already plays on press, so the menu's job is
    // the things a press cannot say.
    episode: [
      ['＋', 'Add to the queue', '', 'queue'],
      ['▸', 'Play next', '', 'queue'],
      ['--'],
      ['▶\uFE0E', 'Play this episode', '', ''],
      ['⧉', 'Copy link', '', ''],
      ['↗', 'Open the episode', '', '']
    ],
    // The CATALOGUE ⋮ is a buy control first. Someone on the index wants the
    // record without opening it — "before I go back to lunch" — so the formats
    // and their prices are the top of the menu and the add actions sit directly
    // under them. '$$' expands to one row per BUY variant (see ctxRowsFor), so
    // the menu and the buy sheet can never disagree on price. The album menu's
    // tail — follow the artist, Instagram, their own site, copy link — is
    // deliberately absent: none of it belongs on a quick-buy path.
    catalogue: [
      ['§ Buy'],
      ['$$'],
      ['--'],
      ['＋', 'Add the record to the queue', '', 'queue'],
      ['▸', 'Play next', '', 'queue'],
      ['＋', 'Add the record to a playlist', '▸', ''],
      ['＋', 'Add to my constellations', '', ''],
      ['--'],
      // ⚠ 'artistPage', NOT 'album' (2026-09-11, engineering: "wrong in all 64
      // places and right in none, so it was never drift"). Two table rows
      // rendered across 32 shells. The row says Go to artist and opened the
      // RECORD — the action it wanted already exists and is used correctly by
      // 'Go to the artist page' in the artist menu.
      ['◈', 'Go to artist', '', 'artistPage'],
      // ── ASK 2 · YES, AND IN THE ALBUM MENU'S POSITION (2026-09-05) ────────
      // `album:` and `episode:` both carry a Copy row and `catalogue:` did
      // not — and the catalogue grid is where a visitor meets a record they
      // might want to send, so it was the surface that needed it most. Same
      // wording, same glyph, same slot (after the navigation rows, before the
      // Listen-elsewhere section), because a menu that differs between
      // surfaces has to be re-learned on each.
      ['⧉', 'Copy a link to this record', '', ''],
      ['--'],
      ['§ Listen elsewhere'],
      ['◎', 'Bandcamp', '↗', ''],
      ['◎', 'Spotify', '↗', ''],
      ['◎', 'Apple Music', '↗', '']
    ],
    album: [
      ['＋', 'Add the record to the queue', '', 'queue'],
      ['▸', 'Play next', '', 'queue'],
      ['＋', 'Add the record to a playlist', '▸', ''],
      // ⚠ 'artistPage', NOT 'album' (2026-09-11, engineering: "wrong in all 64
      // places and right in none, so it was never drift"). Two table rows
      // rendered across 32 shells. The row says Go to artist and opened the
      // RECORD — the action it wanted already exists and is used correctly by
      // 'Go to the artist page' in the artist menu.
      ['◈', 'Go to artist', '', 'artistPage'],
      // THE MENU KEEPS ITS PROMISE (2026-09-04, r5 ask 5): every ⋮ tooltip on
      // this surface says "copy a link" and no menu had a row for it.
      // ctxRowsFor's /^Copy/ test already routes the label to share.copy.
      ['⧉', 'Copy a link to this record', '', ''],
      ['--'],
      ['§ Listen elsewhere'],
      ['◎', 'Bandcamp', '↗', ''],
      ['◎', 'Spotify', '↗', ''],
      ['◎', 'Apple Music', '↗', ''],
      ['◎', 'YouTube', '↗', ''],
      ['--'],
      ['§ Follow the artist'],
      ['◍', 'Instagram', '↗', ''],
      ['◍', 'Their own site', '↗', '']
    ],
    // The ARTIST ⋮ — on a photo in the index, not on a record. So it opens with
    // the artist's own music and their place in the map, then where to hear
    // more. No formats for the artist, but a way INTO the records, which beats
    // a follow link.
    artistTile: [
      ['▶︎', 'Play top tracks', '', 'queue'],
      ['＋', 'Add the artist to the queue', '', 'queue'],
      ['◈', 'Go to the artist page', '', 'artistPage'],
      // ⚠ WITHHELD ON A PHONE (2026-09-14): phoneNoMap() drops this row in
      // ctxRowsFor. Offering a map entry on a device with no map and then
      // redirecting is a worse answer than not offering it — the same
      // reasoning that took Grid | Map out of the phone header.
      ['⤢', 'Open in the map', '', 'artistMap'],
      ['--'],
      ['◉', 'Buy their records', '▸', 'artistPage'],
      ['--'],
      ['§ Listen elsewhere'],
      ['◎', 'Spotify', '↗', ''],
      ['◎', 'Bandcamp', '↗', ''],
      ['◎', 'Apple Music', '↗', ''],
      ['--'],
      ['§ Follow the artist'],
      ['◍', 'Instagram', '↗', ''],
      ['◍', 'Their own site', '↗', '']
    ],
    track: [
      ['＋', 'Add to the queue', '', 'queue'],
      ['▸', 'Play next', '', 'queue'],
      ['＋', 'Add to a playlist', '▸', ''],
      // The per-track Copy row (2026-09-04, ledger 7): the tooltip promised it
      // on this surface too. ctxRowsFor's /^Copy/ routes it to share.copy.
      ['⧉', 'Copy link', '', ''],

    hero: [

  CTX_W = 300;

  // ── Curator map ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  // Both volumes, every name, from acrossthehorizon.space: the curator index
  // gives the sixteen curators, their drop numbers and their tracks; each
  // curator page names the two artists they introduced. Volume Two drops 1–6
  // are read from those pages and are drawn SOLID. Volume One's pairs are read
  // off the reference map's own shape and are drawn DASHED until its data
  // confirms them — except Luke Schneider → Kyle Hamlett Duo / Marisa Anderson

  // photoFor() is the only way to
