  // One renderer, four configurations. MODES holds the geometry; everything
  // else — faces, names, elbow wires, crossing links, selection dimming — is
  // shared, so the four layouts can never drift apart.
  //   dir 'h' = curator above its invitees · dir 'v' = curator left of them
  MODES = {
    grid:     { dir: 'h', perRowFn: (W) => (W >= 1980 ? 6 : W >= 1660 ? 5 : 4), colsPerVol: 1, chunk: 8, c: 70, i: 58, stagger: 0, gap: 18 },
    vertical: { dir: 'v', perRowFn: () => 1, colsPerVol: 1, chunk: 8, c: 58, i: 46, gap: 40 },
    oneline:  { dir: 'h', perRowFn: () => 8, colsPerVol: 1, chunk: 8, c: 56, i: 46, stagger: 74, gap: 10 },
    drift:    { dir: 'free', kind: 'drift' },
    spine:    { dir: 'free', kind: 'spine' }
  };

  // Free-form relationship maps. Both fit a horizontal rectangle: one band per
  // volume, eight curators across, everything absolutely placed from one set of
  // numbers so the shapes stay honest.
  //   drift  curators ride a slow wave; artists trail on curved tethers
  //   spine  one line per volume; artists sit off it, above and below
  // Connections you draw yourself. Stored as [from, to] name pairs — the same
  // shape you can paste back to me in bulk — and kept in localStorage so a
  // reload does not lose them.
  // Every connection lives here: [from, to, note]. Add the other thirty to this
  // list and each one routes itself — no hand placement, no drawing.
  // Every connection on the map: the Marisa Anderson crossing plus the
  // client's CSV — re-baked with CRLF endings stripped; the first bake let
  // every row fail its regex and shipped the failure markers as data.
  // Every connection on the map — the client's full list
  // (uploads/artistconnections-a9821dcb.csv), plus the Marisa crossing.
  // Every connection on the map — the client's full list
  // (uploads/artistconnections-a9821dcb.csv), plus the Marisa crossing.
  // Every connection on the map — the client's full list
  // (uploads/artistconnections-a9821dcb.csv, 55 rows), plus the Marisa crossing.
  // Every connection on the map — the client's full list
  // (uploads/artistconnections-a3c060aa.csv, 52 rows). Shape is
  // [artist1, artist2, artist3, note] — artist3 is '' for a two-person note.
  // ── Drops, newest first (frame 15e) ──────────────────────────────────────
  // One row per drop: the three covers, the three tracks, the curator line. The
  // landing card shows the newest; the catalogue page shows every drop in both
  // volumes, lazy-loaded a few at a time.
  // An artist's newest record in PAIRS, or null. The bridge from name-keyed
  // surfaces (ATH drops, the artists mosaic) into the record-keyed hover chain.
  // ── A RECORD ID RESOLVES THROUGH A SUPPLIED MAP (2026-09-04, F1) ─────────
  // PAIR_IDS is the record id per index (slot catalogue § 7.3) — "the join
  // between a star and its record, supplied, never derived". Every table keyed
  // by record id comes through here, so no surface has to search PAIRS.
  // ── C1 · IMAGES ARRIVE AS THEY APPROACH THE VIEWPORT (2026-09-04) ────────
  // Every wall painted its whole set on mount — 47 portraits on the artists
  // wall, every cover on the volumes page — a multi-megabyte burst that makes a
  // phone trickle even though each asset is small and fast. These are
  // background layers rather than <img>, so loading="lazy" cannot help: the
  // fetch starts when the style lands. So the style lands WITHOUT the image and
  // an IntersectionObserver fills it 300px before the tile arrives, once.
  //
  // A callback ref rather than a data attribute, because renderVals returns
  // style objects and a ref is the one handle that survives them. The tile's
  // own hatch or panel colour stays in the style, so an unfilled tile is the
  // designed empty rather than a hole and nothing reflows as images arrive.
  // No IntersectionObserver in the engine → everything fills immediately, so
  // the wall is never worse than it was before this existed.
  // ── ⚠ THE GUARD IS THE URL, NOT A BOOLEAN (2026-09-10) ───────────────────
  // Engineering, from a live audit: 218 of 313 artist tiles across the five
  // sites painted a DIFFERENT artist's photograph. The Necks showed Jessica
  // Roch; on ATH "Meg Baird" was drawn as hammock.jpg. Their diagnosis was
  // exact and their data was clean.
  //
  // The fault was here, and it was mine. This guard was one-shot PER DOM NODE:
  //
  //     if (!el || el.__saLazy) return; el.__saLazy = 1;
  //
  // and sc-for keys its children POSITIONALLY, so when the mosaic repacks React
  // reuses the node at that position for a different artist. The name and the
  // href are holes and update; the background is painted IMPERATIVELY by this
  // closure, which holds the OLD url — and the guard returned before it could
  // be corrected. Nothing threw, nothing 404'd, every tile looked healthy.
  //
  // ⚠ THIS IS THE GENERAL SHAPE OF THE BUG, NOT AN ARTISTS-WALL BUG. Any
  // imperative paint keyed on "have I run for this node" is wrong in a list
  // that reorders; the question is always "have I run for this VALUE". The
  // covers, the drops and the volumes walls all come through this one function,
  // so they were all exposed and are all fixed by this.
  //
  // Three states, and the middle one is the one that was missing:
  //   same node, same layer   → nothing to do
  //   same node, NEW layer    → repaint. Immediately if it was already showing
  //                             the old face (it is on screen; a wrong face is
  //                             worse than an early fetch), otherwise just
  //                             re-arm the pending fill.
  //   fresh node              → observe and fill as before.
  //
  // ⚠ __saBase, captured once, is what makes a repaint possible. fill()
  // PREPENDS onto the element's inline background, so after one fill that
  // string already contains the old image; prepending again would stack faces
  // with the stale one still visible underneath. The base is the background as
  // the STYLE declared it — the tile's own hatch or panel colour — so every
  // repaint composes from the design, never from the previous paint.
  //
  // Engineering asked whether we would rather fix this or give sc-for a stable
  // key. Both, ideally, but this one is ours and it is the real defect: a
  // stable key would hide it here while leaving every other imperative paint
  // in the file wrong the moment a list reorders. __saLazy / __saFilled / __saFill
  // keep their names so their diagnostic script still reads them.
  lazyRef(url, focus) {
    if (!url) return null;
    const layer = 'url(' + this.res(url) + ') center ' + (focus || 'center') + '/cover no-repeat';
    return (el) => {
      if (!el) return;
      if (el.__saSrc === layer) return;
      const reused = el.__saSrc != null;
      el.__saSrc = layer;
      el.__saLazy = 1;
      if (el.__saBase == null) el.__saBase = el.style.background || '';
      const fill = () => {
        el.__saFilled = layer;
        el.style.background = layer + (el.__saBase ? ', ' + el.__saBase : '');
      };
      el.__saFill = fill;
      // Reused a node that is already showing someone else's face: repaint now.
      if (reused && el.__saFilled) { fill(); return; }
      // Reused a node still waiting on the observer: __saFill above is already
      // the new closure and the element is still observed, so nothing else.
      if (reused) return;
      if (typeof IntersectionObserver !== 'function') { fill(); return; }
      if (!this._lazyIO) {
        this._lazyIO = new IntersectionObserver((entries, obs) => {
          entries.forEach(en => {
            if (!en.isIntersecting) return;
            obs.unobserve(en.target);
            if (en.target.__saFill) en.target.__saFill();
          });
        }, { rootMargin: '300px 0px' });
      }
      this._lazyIO.observe(el);
    };
  }
  // The two broadcast tables, new name first and the old one as the alias
  // (D3). One reader, so a half-migrated feed cannot show two different answers
  // on the same page.
  broadcastHomes() {
    const n = this.data('BROADCAST_HOMES');
    return (n && n.length) ? n : (this.data('RADIO_HOMES') || []);
  }
  broadcastRecent() {
    const n = this.data('BROADCAST_RECENT');
    return (n && n.length) ? n : (this.data('RADIO_BEFORE') || []);
  }
  recIndexOf(id) {
    if (id == null) return null;
    const ids = this.data('PAIR_IDS') || [];
    for (let i = 0; i < ids.length; i++) if (ids[i] === id) return i;
    return null;
  }
  recFor(name) {
    const P = this.data('PAIRS');
    for (let q = 0; q < P.length; q++) if (P[q] && P[q][0] === name) return q;
    // ATH: a face on the curator wall may have no PAIRS row under their own
    // name — the drop is the record. Find the drop they curated or played on
    // (VOLS) and match its title or its curator into PAIRS. Same chain, one
    // more hop; still null when the site has not supplied the drop.
    let hit = null;
    this.data('VOLS').forEach((v) => (v.drops || []).forEach((d) => {
      if (hit != null) return;
      const on = d[1] === name || (d[3] || []).indexOf(name) > -1;
      if (!on) return;
      for (let q = 0; q < P.length; q++) { const p = P[q]; if (!p) continue; if (p[1] === d[2] || p[0] === d[1] || (String(p[0]).indexOf(name) > -1)) { hit = q; return; } }
    }));
    return hit;
  }
  buildDrops(st) {
        // The same short-phone flag renderVals uses: this builder is called from
        // there, but it cannot see its locals.
        const VP = this.VPS[st.vp] || [1180, 760];
        const tight = VP[0] < 700 && VP[1] < 700;

        // ── THE SIDEBAR READS VOLS_RELEASED; THE FIELD KEEPS VOLS ────────────
        // (2026-09-10, engineering: "the volumes sidebar and the star field
        // share one VOLS. They need different lists: the field shows every seat
        // including unreleased drops (the announcement lineup); the sidebar
        // shows released only… the host already injects both on every arrival.")
        //
        // Right, and it is the better answer than the one I had just built. My
        // gate inferred "public" from whether a track carried a real title —
        // a HEURISTIC, and one that would call an announced-with-titles drop
        // released. VOLS_RELEASED is the host stating the fact, so the fact
        // wins and the inference becomes a fallback for when it is absent.
        //
        // ⚠ ONLY buildDrops MOVES. This builder feeds the release surfaces —
        // the Starmap column's list, the volumes list, the drops card and the
        // counts. The FIELD is drawn by buildMap/buildBands, which read
        // this.data('VOLS') directly and are deliberately untouched: the
        // announcement lineup is the point of the map. athDrop() also stays on
        // VOLS so an announced drop's own page still resolves when linked.
        // ⚠ READ DEFENSIVELY, NOT THROUGH data(). VOLS_RELEASED is new and
        // carries no DEFAULT_ and no classification, and data()'s site-mode
        // guard THROWS on an unclassified data-class name — so routing it
        // through data() would have taken the whole render down on the live
        // site the moment the host did not inject it. Same two sources data()
        // reads, in the same order, minus the throw: present → used, absent →
        // VOLS, which is exactly today's behaviour. Nothing to classify.
        const volsReleased = (this.props && this.props.VOLS_RELEASED)
          || ((typeof window !== 'undefined' && window.__DC_DATA) ? window.__DC_DATA.VOLS_RELEASED : null);
        const all = [];
        (volsReleased || this.data('VOLS')).forEach((v, vi) => (v.drops || []).forEach((d, di) => {
          // d[4] IS THE RELEASED FLAG (2026-09-04). The landing shows the
          // volumes listing and the curator map from this one dataset, so the
          // Latest Drop module cannot rely on the page split that keeps
          // unreleased drops out elsewhere — it reads this flag. Absent → 1,
          // so older data behaves as before.
          all.push({ vi: vi, di: di, num: d[0], curator: d[1], track: d[2], guests: d[3] || [], released: d[4] === undefined || d[4] === null ? 1 : d[4] });
        }));
        all.reverse();
        // Sixteen drops is a short list: load them all and let it scroll.
        const limit = all.length;
        const mk = (r, i) => {
          const names = [r.curator].concat(r.guests).slice(0, 3);
          const pending = /pending/i.test(r.curator);
          // ── ONE RESOLUTION PER ROW, READ BY BOTH THE GATE AND THE PRESS ─────
          // (2026-09-15) The press resolved its own audio here and the button's
          // style was resolved somewhere else entirely — renderVals'
          // athDeckPlayStyle, off athDrop(st).r — so the two could answer about
          // DIFFERENT DROPS. athLatestDrop is two rows on desktop, anchored at
          // newestReleased(); athDrop(st) answers with VOLS' newest, or with
          // st.dropKey, left over from a release page. Measured on the tool's
          // data: the deck showed Vol. 2 Drops 5 and 4 while the gate was
          // reading Drop 8, which is not on the card at all.
          //
          // ⚠ IT AGREED BY LUCK, NOT BY CONSTRUCTION. All sixteen drops resolve
          // something here (13 by VOLS title, 3 by record), so both gates said
          // "show" and nothing looked wrong — which is why this had to be read
          // rather than seen. Live, one title-pending drop in either position
          // flips it: a pending Drop 8 hides the buttons on two playable rows,
          // and a pending row inherits a playable Drop 8's button and bails on
          // the press.
          //
          // So the gate is not re-derived, it IS the derivation the press uses:
          // the row's record if it has one, else the first public title for the
          // ext channel. Two readers, one fact — a control cannot render for
          // audio its own press will refuse.
          const playRec = this.dropRowRec(r, r.curator);
          const playTitle = playRec != null ? '' : (() => {
            if (r.track && !/pending/i.test(r.track)) return String(r.track).split(' · ')[0];
            const trk = this.athDropTrack('Vol. ' + (r.vi + 1) + ' Drop ' + r.num, 0);
            const raw = trk ? String(trk[1] || '') : '';
            return (raw && !/pending/i.test(raw)) ? raw.split(' · ')[0] : '';
          })();
          const canPlay = playRec != null || !!playTitle;
          return {
            key: 'dr' + r.vi + '-' + r.di,
            title: 'Vol. ' + (r.vi + 1) + ' Drop ' + r.num,
            released: r.released,
            newest: i === 0,
            badgeStyle: i === 0
              ? { padding: '4px 9px', borderRadius: '4px', background: 'rgba(138,106,18,0.14)', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '8px', letterSpacing: '0.16em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--gold)' }
              : { display: 'none' },
            // ── THE COPY TOM STRUCK (ported from V1, 2026-09-09) ────────────────
        // He asked for "and one of the three on it · 2026 · LP · CS · DL" gone
        // and the release date in its place. V2 still carried the whole string
        // verbatim — a copy regression, not a missing feature, and the worse of
        // the two because it reads as deliberate.
        //
        // The date comes from relDateLabel(r.relDate) — a DATA POINTER, so the
        // line appears when engineering fill RELDATE and says nothing until
        // then, rather than printing an invented year. The formats and the track
        // count went because the card is a preview: the release page carries
        // them, and repeating them here was saying twice what the card is not
        // for.
        meta: 'Curated by ' + r.curator + (this.relDateLabel(r.relDate) ? ('  ·  ' + this.relDateLabel(r.relDate)) : ''),
            // The drop title and its covers click into the drop's own page; the
            // track rows go one level deeper, to the track.
            openDrop: () => this.setState({ route: 'ath-release', dropKey: 'd' + r.vi + '-' + r.di, trackIdx: 0, getMusic: null }),
            // The row's OWN play (2026-09-04, DQ-43). "▶ Play the drop" on
            // every drop row bound the top-level startPlayback, so all sixteen
            // rows on the volumes page played the same drop. Resolved through
            // dropRowRec, the same resolver the track rows use, so the row and
            // its tracks can never disagree about what the drop is.
            // ── THE DROP'S CONTROL SHOWS ITS STATE (2026-09-05, Tom) ────────
            // "▶︎ Play the drop" was a literal, so the card's own control never
            // changed while its tracklist rows below it correctly showed ❙❙ and
            // took the gold wash. Same test as the rows (dropRowOn), so the
            // header and the list can never disagree — and on this route there
            // is no transport bar, which makes these two the ONLY places the
            // state can be read.
            playLabel: this.dropRowOn(st, r, r.curator, 0) ? '❙❙ Pause' : '▶\uFE0E Play the drop',
            // The button's own gate, per row, off the derivation above. Mode is
            // read from the RENDER's st rather than this.state (playCtl reads
            // this.state; r76 §6 is the case where those differ — a style that
            // answers for another viewport is the same class of fault as one
            // that answers for another drop, which is what this key just was).
            // 40px, not 38 (2026-09-15 audit). Measured 125×38 on the phone
            // landing, which fails CLAUDE.md's ≥40px touch rule by 2px on a
            // primary control. One value for both widths rather than a narrow
            // branch: 40 is the floor everywhere, and it puts this in line with
            // its siblings — athStrandPlayStyle 44, heroPlayBtnStyle 42, the
            // ATH deck row glyph 40×40.
            playStyle: (!canPlay || !this.playMode(st))
              ? { display: 'none' }
              : {
                  display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', height: '40px', padding: '0 14px',
                  boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '5px', background: 'var(--gold)',
                  color: 'var(--ongold)', fontWeight: 700, cursor: 'pointer'
                },
            // ══ AND THE DROP PLAYS BY EITHER PATH TOO (2026-09-15, Tom: "for
            // the latest drops, there should be a 'Play the drop' button,
            // correct") ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
            // Correct, and it was hidden at BOTH widths — measured. I fixed the
            // TRACK rows to play through playExt when there is no catalogue
            // record and left this one still demanding one, so the deck ended up
            // stating two different rules about the same drop: its tracks were
            // playable and its Play button was not.
            //
            // A drop's play is its FIRST PUBLIC TRACK, which is what track 0
            // means here. So it resolves the same way a row does: the curator's
            // record if there is one, otherwise the ext channel with the drop's
            // own id. preventDefault as well, per r47 §1.
            play: (e) => {
              if (e) {
                if (e.preventDefault) e.preventDefault();
                if (e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();
              }
              if (this.dropRowOn(st, r, r.curator, 0)) { this.setState({ paused: true }); return; }
              // ⚠ dropPlay IS SET ONLY WHEN SOMETHING WILL ACTUALLY PLAY
              // (2026-09-15, review). It used to be set before the title check,
              // so a press on an unplayable drop still flipped the label to
              // "❙❙ Pause" and then returned — state changed, nothing sounded,
              // no transport. The same fault the playable-term gates exist to
              // prevent, reintroduced by statement ORDER rather than by a
              // missing gate. Nothing is claimed until the claim is true.
              if (playRec != null) {
                this.setState({ dropPlay: 'd' + r.vi + '-' + r.di });
                this.play(playRec, 0);
                return;
              }
              if (!playTitle) return;
              this.setState({ dropPlay: 'd' + r.vi + '-' + r.di });
              this.playExt(this.athExtId(r, 0), r.curator, playTitle);
            },
            // Counted, and the lengths note only while there are no lengths
            // (r5 ask 2). "Three tracks · Lengths pending" was both typed.
            trackCount: (() => {
              const n = names.length;
              return (['zero','one','two','three','four','five'][n] || String(n)) + (n === 1 ? ' track' : ' tracks');
            })(),
            lengthNote: '',
            lengthNoteStyle: { display: 'none' },
            // ── THE COVERS ARE REAL (2026-09-04, ledger 15) ────────────────
            // Every tile drew the hatch unconditionally, so the volumes list
            // and the landing's Latest Drop showed blank squares for drops
            // whose covers exist. Joined to ATH_DROPS by title; each track's
            // own cover is tracks[ci][4]. Absent → the hatch, exactly as before.
            covers: names.map((n, ci) => {
              const trk = this.athDropTrack('Vol. ' + (r.vi + 1) + ' Drop ' + r.num, ci);
              const cover = trk ? trk[4] : '';
              return {
              key: 'cv' + ci,
              // Lazy, same as the artists wall (C1): the volumes page carries
              // every drop's three covers, which is the file's largest burst.
              style: {
                position: 'relative', width: '100%', aspectRatio: '1', boxSizing: 'border-box',
                border: '1px solid ' + (ci === 0 ? 'rgba(138,106,18,0.55)' : 'var(--hair)'),
                background: cover
                  ? 'var(--tile)'
                  : 'repeating-linear-gradient(135deg, var(--tile) 0 8px, transparent 8px 16px)',
                cursor: 'pointer'
              },
              coverRef: this.lazyRef(cover),
            // ── THE COVER SHOWS ITS QUOTE ON HOVER (2026-09-06, Tom) ──────
            // "Every single, every cover, should have a mouseover quote." The
            // cover dims and the artist's own words rise over it — so the quote
            // is attached to the thing it is about, and reading the drop means
            // moving across three covers and hearing three tracks while three
            // artists tell you what they made. Hover audio is already armed on
            // both these surfaces; this is the same gesture given something to
            // read.
            //
            // Per TRACK, not per drop: the quote belongs to the single. Source
            // order in athQuoteForTrack — the track's own key first, then the
            // artist's name.
            //
            // Small type on purpose: a whole quote has to fit inside a square
            // without scrolling or clipping, and 11/12px at 1.5 holds roughly
            // forty words in the tile at its usual sizes. Long quotes clamp
            // rather than overflow — the cover is not a reading surface, it is
            // an invitation to the track page where the quote is set properly.
            quote: this.athQuoteForTrack(r, ci, n),
            quoteWrapStyle: (() => {
              const q = this.athQuoteForTrack(r, ci, n);
              const on = q && st.dropHover === ('d' + r.vi + '-' + r.di + '-' + ci);
              return on
                ? {
                    position: 'absolute', left: 0, right: 0, top: 0, bottom: 0, zIndex: 2,
                    display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center',
                    padding: '14px', boxSizing: 'border-box',
                    // The cover reads THROUGH this rather than being replaced:
                    // you should still know which single you are reading about.
                    background: 'rgba(247,245,239,0.93)',
                    pointerEvents: 'none'
                  }
                : { display: 'none' };
            })(),
            quoteTextStyle: {
              fontSize: '11.5px', lineHeight: 1.5, color: 'var(--ink)',
              fontStyle: 'italic', textAlign: 'center', textWrap: 'pretty',
              display: '-webkit-box', WebkitLineClamp: 9, WebkitBoxOrient: 'vertical', overflow: 'hidden'
            },
              numStyle: { position: 'absolute', left: '8px', bottom: '8px', display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '5px', padding: '3px 7px', background: 'rgba(255,255,255,0.92)', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.1em', color: ci === 0 ? 'var(--gold)' : 'rgba(23,25,27,0.62)' },
              // THE CAPTION IS GATED ON THE COVER (2026-09-04, r5 ask 2). It
              // printed "Cover pending" over loaded artwork, because the note
              // was markup and the cover became data one drop later.
              pendNote: cover ? '' : 'Cover pending',
              num: '0' + (ci + 1),
              pendStyle: cover
                ? { display: 'none' }
                : { position: 'absolute', right: '8px', bottom: '8px', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '8px', letterSpacing: '0.1em', textTransform: 'uppercase', fontStyle: 'italic', color: 'var(--ink40)' },
              click: () => this.setState({ route: 'ath-track', dropKey: 'd' + r.vi + '-' + r.di, trackIdx: ci, getMusic: null }),
              // ── NO PLAY BUTTON ON A COVER EITHER (2026-09-05, Tom) ─────────
              // ⚠ ALSO MINE, from the same r6 pass. The drops card and the
              // volumes page are cover surfaces: hovering a cover plays it,
              // which is why hover audio is armed by default on both. A ▶ on
              // top of that is a second control for something already
              // happening — and on the deck there is no transport to press it
              // into. The cover stays a door to the track page; the sound is
              // the hover's.
              //
              // ══ AND BOTH HALVES OF THAT ARE FALSE ON A PHONE (2026-09-14,
              // Tom: "the ATH landing page on mobile now is in player mode, not
              // in hover mode... seeing the play buttons, I'm not seeing that
              // change") ════════════════════════════════════════════════════
              // The reasoning above was sound for a cover surface with hover
              // audio armed and no transport. A phone now has NEITHER: hover is
              // gone, and player is its one audio mode, so the transport is
              // always there to press a track into.
              //
              // So the constant was doing the same damage on ATH that the
              // hover-gated play control did in the catalogue: a cover surface
              // with no way to sound a record. SAME FAULT, different mechanism
              // — there the control was revealed by something a phone does not
              // have; here it was never built at all.
              //
              // NOT playCtl(). That reads playMode, which is true on a DESKTOP
              // in player mode too, and on a desktop the 09-05 ruling still
              // holds: hover audio is armed on ATH by default, so the cover is
              // already sounding under the cursor. The gate is the VIEWPORT,
              // not the mode — this control exists where there is no hover to
              // replace it. _narrowNow(), because athColGroups is a builder and
              // has no `narrow` in scope.
              //
              // ══ AND THE 09-05 RULING STANDS. I OVERTURNED IT ON A PREMISE I
              // NEVER MEASURED (2026-09-14, review) ═══════════════════════════
              // I put a 44px play control here arguing that a phone has
              // "NEITHER" hover nor a transport, so the cover had "no way to
              // sound a record". The first half is true. The second is exactly
              // backwards, and it is measurable on this very screen: THE DECK'S
              // TRACK ROWS ALREADY CARRY WORKING PLAY CONTROLS — 342 × 51px,
              // cursor:pointer, their own handler, a ▶︎ inside, sitting
              // directly beneath these covers and fully reachable by a finger.
              //
              // So the covers are not this deck's play surface; the rows are,
              // and they always were. A control here would be a SECOND control
              // for the same act on a 109px tile — precisely what the 09-05
              // ruling forbade in the sentence above: "a ▶ on top of that is a
              // second control for something already happening".
              //
              // What changed on a phone is which affordance carries the act,
              // not whether one exists. Hover carried it; the rows carry it now.
              // The catalogue was a real fault because a grid card has no row
              // beneath it — there the cover IS the only surface. Here it is
              // not, and I reasoned from the catalogue to ATH without checking
              // whether the premise transferred.
              //
              // Kept as a false constant so the reason survives, as it did
              // before — and now records why the phone does not change it.
              playStyle: { display: 'none' },
              // preventDefault as well as stopPropagation - the r47 section 1
              // lesson engineering re-taught on 09-14. These covers are anchors
              // too, and stopPropagation only stops the handler that would have
              // cancelled the default.
              play: (e) => {
                if (e) {
                  if (e.preventDefault) e.preventDefault();
                  if (e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();
                }
                const rec = this.recFor(n);
                if (rec != null) this.play(rec, ci);
              },
              // Hover audio on ATH covers: the cover is that artist's record.
              enter: () => this.hoverEnter({ dropHover: 'd' + r.vi + '-' + r.di + '-' + ci, catHover: this.recFor(n), catHoverName: n, catHoverTitle: (ci === 0 ? r.track : '') }),
              leave: () => this.setState({ dropHover: null, catHover: null, catHoverName: null, catHoverTitle: null })
              };
            }),
            tracks: names.map((n, ti) => ({
              key: 'tk' + ti,
              // ── A HOVERED COVER LIGHTS ITS OWN ROW (2026-09-07, Tom) ───────
              // "When I mouse over the album cover, the track list on the side
              // should be alive… highlight and pause button, and then as soon as
              // the mouseover is left, it stops."
              //
              // The cover already previews the track — hover audio is armed on
              // ATH — and already knows WHICH track it is: st.dropHover is
              // 'd<vi>-<di>-<ci>', set by the cover's own enter/leave for the
              // quote overlay. The rows simply were not reading it. So a row is
              // live on EITHER of two facts:
              //
              //   dropRowOn   the transport is actually playing this row
              //   dropRowHov  a cover is under the cursor and it is THIS row's
              //
              // Both give the gold wash and the ❙❙ glyph, because from the
              // visitor's side they are one fact: this is the track you are
              // hearing. The hover one clears itself — leaving the cover clears
              // dropHover, so the row goes quiet with the sound, which is the
              // "as soon as the mouseover is left, it stops" half.
              //
              // It also answers the rest of the complaint: the quote overlay
              // covers the cover's own artist name, so you could not see who and
              // what at once. The row names them, beside it, lit.
              // ⚠ THE GLYPH COLUMN IS A TOUCH TARGET ON NARROW (2026-09-15).
              // 14px was sized for a 10px mono glyph under a cursor. CLAUDE.md:
              // "≥40px on anything interactive — including bare glyphs, which
              // need a box." The row is 51px tall, so only the WIDTH was short;
              // widening the column to 40 costs the name column 26 of its ~258
              // and nothing wraps. Desktop keeps 14: a cursor does not need 40,
              // and the tighter column is what lets three tracks fit the card.
              rowStyle: {
                display: 'grid', gridTemplateColumns: this._narrowNow() ? '18px 40px 1fr 22px' : '18px 14px 1fr 22px', alignItems: 'center', gap: '10px',
                padding: tight ? '5px 8px 5px 0' : '8px 8px 8px 0', borderTop: '1px solid var(--hair)',
                borderBottom: ti === 2 ? '1px solid var(--hair)' : 'none',
                background: (this.dropRowOn(st, r, n, ti) || this.dropRowHov(st, r, ti))
                  ? 'rgba(138,106,18,0.14)'
                  : (ti === 0 ? 'rgba(138,106,18,0.07)' : 'transparent'),
                // 120ms so the wash arrives with the cursor rather than snapping:
                // the row is following a gesture, not reporting a click.
                transition: 'background 120ms linear',
                cursor: 'pointer'
              },
              numStyle: { fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '10px', paddingLeft: '8px', color: ti === 0 ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--ink55)' },
              num: '0' + (ti + 1),
              // ── THE ROW SHOWS WHAT IS SOUNDING (2026-09-04) ───────────────
              // The glyph was a fixed ▶ and the row only navigated, so on the
              // drops card and the volumes page a track could be playing with
              // nothing on the page saying which — and with the transport
              // suppressed on those routes the row is the ONLY place that
              // state can live. "On" is this record playing AND this row's
              // index: the glyph reads ❙❙ and the row takes the gold wash.
              // ⚠ THE DROP IS PART OF THE IDENTITY. Record + row index alone
              // lit two rows at once: the volumes page lists sixteen drops, and
              // an artist who appears in two of them matched in both at the
              // same index. dropPlay records WHICH drop the transport was
              // started from, so exactly one row can be on.
              // V2 · in HOVER mode there is no ▶︎ on a row: the only glyph is the
              // ❙❙ of the track under the cursor. The row is still the door to the
              // track page — that is navigation, not playback — so the row keeps
              // its click; what goes is the play affordance.
              playGlyph: (this.dropRowOn(st, r, n, ti) || this.dropRowHov(st, r, ti)) ? '❙❙' : (this.playMode(st) ? '▶\uFE0E' : ''),
              // ── ABSENT WHERE THE ROW RESOLVES NO RECORD (2026-09-09) ────────
              // Six of these painted on the drops panel and none of them could
              // start audio: dropRowRec is null for every row of that drop, so
              // play() correctly refuses and the visitor presses a ▶︎ that does
              // nothing. The drop's own button covered ONE of seven play
              // controls on that panel (then athDeckPlayStyle, now the row's
              // playStyle); these are the other six.
              //
              // Same rule as athTrkSiblings and the curator-map circles. The ROW
              // keeps its click either way — opening the track page is
              // navigation, not playback, and that works without a record.
              // ⚠ THIS CELL IS A GRID COLUMN, SO IT CANNOT LEAVE (2026-09-09,
              // Tom: on Volumes "the track list is just not showing", and the
              // play buttons "don't appear and disappear"). The row is a
              // four-column grid — 18px 14px 1fr 22px — and a display:none child
              // is not placed at all, so in hover mode (and on every row with no
              // record) the remaining three items each slid one column left: the
              // name landed in the 14px glyph column and printed one letter per
              // line. Both reports are that one fault.
              //
              // So the CONTROL still goes — no glyph, nothing to press, which is
              // hover mode's rule — but the CELL stays. visibility, not display,
              // is the difference between "this control is absent" and "this
              // column is absent", and only the first was ever meant.
              //
              // ══ THIS IS THE ATH DECK'S PLAY CONTROL, AND IT IS THE ONLY ONE
              // (2026-09-15, Tom: "the ATH landing page in a mobile view… there
              // are no play buttons") ══════════════════════════════════════════
              // Two corrections to what the file said before, both measured:
              //
              // 1 · IT WAS NOT A TOUCH TARGET. fontSize:10px with no box renders
              //     14 × 12. A previous note asserted these rows were "working
              //     play controls… fully reachable by a finger", and on the
              //     strength of that I reverted a working 44px control on the
              //     covers above. The wiring was right; the box was never there.
              //     Fixed: 40 × 40 on narrow, in a 40px column.
              //
              // 2 · IT LOOKS INERT IN THE DESIGN TOOL, AND THAT IS HONEST.
              //     dropRowRec → recFor(name) matches an ATH artist against
              //     PAIRS, and the tool's placeholder PAIRS is the Northern Spy
              //     catalogue — Balmorhea, Rachel Grimes and Takeshi Nishimoto
              //     are not in it, so NO ATH row resolves a record and every
              //     glyph is correctly withheld. On the live site PAIRS carries
              //     all 496 records including ATH's, and they light.
              //     ⚠ SO DO NOT "FIX" THIS BY DROPPING THE PLAYABLE TERM. A
              //     control that renders and does nothing is worse than one
              //     that is absent, and the term is what keeps that true.
              playStyle: (() => {
                const box = this._narrowNow()
                  ? { width: '40px', height: '40px', boxSizing: 'border-box', display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center', fontSize: '13px', marginLeft: '-2px' }
                  : { fontSize: '10px' };
                // ══ PLAYABLE BY EITHER PATH (2026-09-15, Tom: "why are the
                // play, drop, and play buttons on the tracks missing on desktop
                // too? … Neither have to play the tracks.") ═══════════════════
                // Measured at both widths: all six row glyphs visibility:hidden,
                // INCLUDING Alan Sparhawk's "Temple Towers" and Chuck Johnson's
                // "Spiralic" — tracks with real titles. So this was never a
                // missing-data story, which is what I told him twice.
                //
                // THE GATE ASKED THE WRONG QUESTION. It asked "does this track
                // have a PAIRS record", because play(rec, track) needs a
                // catalogue index. ATH's drop tracks are not in PAIRS — the
                // table is the label catalogue — so every ATH row failed a test
                // it could never pass, at every width, for ever.
                //
                // But a PAIRS index is not what playing needs. This file already
                // plays things that have no catalogue row: playExt(id, under,
                // title) carries the broadcast strands, sets extPlay, and emits
                // queue.played with ext:{id,under,title} — and the engineering
                // contract already says "when ext is non-null, ignore rec". A
                // drop track has everything that path wants: an artist, a title
                // and a stable id.
                //
                // So the question becomes "is there anything to play", answered
                // as: a PAIRS record if one exists, otherwise the ext channel.
                // The playable TERM stays — a row with no title is still withheld
                // — because a control that renders and does nothing is worse
                // than no control, which is the lesson this gate was added for.
                const rowRec = this.dropRowRec(r, n);
                // ⚠ THE SAME EXPRESSION titleText USES, INCLUDING r.track. My
                // first pass read only athDropTrack, so track 0 — whose title
                // lives on the DROP (r.track, e.g. "Temple Towers") — came back
                // empty and the row stayed hidden. The row displays titleText;
                // a gate that decides whether the row is playable has to read
                // the same field the row prints, or the two disagree about
                // whether a track exists.
                const rowTitle = (() => {
                  if (ti === 0 && r.track && !/pending/i.test(r.track)) return String(r.track).split(' · ')[0];
                  const trk = this.athDropTrack('Vol. ' + (r.vi + 1) + ' Drop ' + r.num, ti);
                  const raw = trk ? String(trk[1] || '') : '';
                  return (raw && !/pending/i.test(raw)) ? raw.split(' · ')[0] : '';
                })();
                const rowPlayable = rowRec != null || !!rowTitle;
                const shown = this.playCtl(
                  !rowPlayable
                    ? { display: 'none' }
                    : Object.assign({}, box, {
                        cursor: 'pointer',
                        color: (this.dropRowOn(st, r, n, ti) || this.dropRowHov(st, r, ti)) ? 'var(--gold)' : (ti === 0 ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--ink55)')
                      })
                );
                return shown.display === 'none'
                  ? Object.assign({}, box, { visibility: 'hidden', pointerEvents: 'none' })
                  : shown;
              })(),
              // The glyph plays in place and stops propagation; the ROW still
              // opens the track page. Two affordances in one row — the
              // catalogue grid's rule, applied here.
              playClick: (e) => {
                // preventDefault as well — the r47 §1 lesson. stopPropagation
                // stops the handler that would have cancelled a wrapping
                // anchor's default; it does not stop the default.
                if (e) {
                  if (e.preventDefault) e.preventDefault();
                  if (e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();
                }
                if (this.dropRowOn(st, r, n, ti)) { this.setState({ paused: true }); return; }
                // dropRowRec, not recFor: an invited artist usually has no
                // record of their own, and the drop's record is the one that
                // carries their track (2026-09-04).
                const rec = this.dropRowRec(r, n);
                this.setState({ dropPlay: 'd' + r.vi + '-' + r.di });
                // ⚠ AND WHEN THERE IS NO RECORD IT PLAYS THROUGH ext, NOT
                // NOTHING (2026-09-15). This branch was `this.play()` — no
                // arguments, which is the STOP call — so a press on an ATH row
                // with no PAIRS entry silently stopped playback. The glyph was
                // hidden, so nobody could reach it; unhiding it above without
                // fixing this would have shipped six controls that stop the
                // music.
                //
                // The id is the drop and the track: 'ath-d<vi>-<di>-<ti>'. A new
                // namespace on an EXISTING channel, so nothing new to wire
                // beyond resolving it — written into the manifest rather than
                // left to be discovered.
                if (rec == null) {
                  const title = (() => {
                    if (ti === 0 && r.track && !/pending/i.test(r.track)) return String(r.track).split(' · ')[0];
                    const trk = this.athDropTrack('Vol. ' + (r.vi + 1) + ' Drop ' + r.num, ti);
                    const raw = trk ? String(trk[1] || '') : '';
                    return (raw && !/pending/i.test(raw)) ? raw.split(' · ')[0] : '';
                  })();
                  if (!title) return;
                  this.playExt(this.athExtId(r, ti), n, title);
                  return;
                }
                this.play(rec, ti);
              },
              // ── CREDIT AND TITLE FROM ONE ROW (2026-09-04, interim 2) ──────
          // `names` is [curator, ...guests] from VOLS, paired to the title by
          // position — two tables, two orders, and a drop whose guest list is
          // ordered differently credits the wrong artist with the track. The
          // credit now comes from ATH_DROPS[].tracks[ti][0], the same row that
          // carries the title, so the two cannot disagree. VOLS' name is the
          // fallback only while a drop is absent from ATH_DROPS.
          name: (() => {
            const trk = this.athDropTrack('Vol. ' + (r.vi + 1) + ' Drop ' + r.num, ti);
            const who = (trk && trk[0]) ? trk[0] : n;
            return who + (ti === 0 ? '  · curator' : '  · invited');
          })(),
              // THE TITLES EXIST (2026-09-04, ledger 18). VOLS carries one
              // track title — the curator's — so the two invited artists on
              // every drop printed "Title pending": two of every three rows.
              // ATH_DROPS[].tracks[ti][1] has all of them.
              titleText: (() => {
                if (ti === 0 && r.track && !/pending/i.test(r.track)) return r.track;
                const trk = this.athDropTrack('Vol. ' + (r.vi + 1) + ' Drop ' + r.num, ti);
                return (trk && trk[1]) ? trk[1] : 'Title pending';
              })(),
              click: () => this.setState({ route: 'ath-track', dropKey: 'd' + r.vi + '-' + r.di, trackIdx: ti, getMusic: null, dropPlay: 'd' + r.vi + '-' + r.di }),
              enter: () => this.hoverEnter({ catHover: this.recFor(n), catHoverName: n, catHoverTitle: (ti === 0 ? r.track : '') }),
              leave: () => this.setState({ catHover: null, catHoverName: null, catHoverTitle: null }),
              // The row goes to the track page; the ⋮ is the ITEM control and
              // opens in place. It had no handler at all here, so it looked
              // interactive and did nothing.
              dotsStyle: { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center', width: '26px', height: '34px', flexShrink: 0, boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '4px', fontSize: '15px', color: 'var(--ink70)', cursor: 'pointer' },
              dots: this.openCtx('track', null)
            }))
          };
        };
        return { all: all.map(mk), latest: all.slice(0, 1).map(mk), shown: all.slice(0, limit).map(mk), total: all.length, more: limit < all.length };
  }

  // Every name that appears more than once on the map, with each appearance's
  // volume, drop and role — so a lit name can be narrowed to a single face.
  dupeIndex() {
    if (this._dupes) return this._dupes;
    const idx = {};
    (this.data('VOLS') || []).forEach((v, vi) => (v.drops || []).forEach((d, di) => {
      const add = (n, isC) => {
        const k = this.slug(n);
        (idx[k] = idx[k] || []).push({ vi: vi, di: di, isC: isC });
      };
      add(d[1], true);
      (d[3] || []).forEach(g => add(g, false));
    }));
    this._dupes = idx;
    return idx;
  }
  /* ══ DC-DEFAULTS:START run=13 keep=CONNECTIONS ══ */

    // The one self-crossing. Names are QUALIFIED as name:volume:drop:role so

    ["Mary Lattimore", "Meg Baird", "", "Lattimore and Baird made the duo album Ghost Forests in 2018 and have guested on each other's solo records for over a decade."],
