  // Not a product — built to order, so it sits apart from the four rather than
  // making a fifth card and breaking the even row.
  DEFAULT_GRP_CUSTOM = ['Custom Apps', 'Bespoke', 'Software built to order for music companies and artists.'];

  /* ══ DC-DEFAULTS:END run=28 keep=GRP_CONTACT,GRP_LABELS,GRP_SERVICES,GRP_PRODUCTS,GRP_CUSTOM ══ */
  renderVals() {
    const st = this.state;
    // ── ONE AUTHORITY FOR GRID AND MAP (2026-09-08) ──────────────────────────
    // Hoisted out of the returned object so BOTH the exposed narrow pair and the
    // new View dropdown call the same two functions. Defining a second copy
    // inside viewModes would have been two answers to "what does Grid mean" —
    // and Grid's answer is not simple (it reads the star column's DERIVED view,
    // see below), so a copy would have drifted the first time that changed.
    // Both view switches mute hover-open first: each one slides the header run
    // sideways by the drawer's width (see panelEnter). Armed BEFORE the state
    // change, so the mute is already in place when the transition starts.
    // ── MAP VIEW OPENS THE LANDING DECK (2026-09-09, Tom) ───────────────────
    // "I go to the Across the Horizon landing page in grid view, hit Map View,
    // it shows me releases inside. It needs to show me the landing page ported
    // to the sidebar." ATH's column IS its landing page; releases is one of the
    // other labels' answers and was never ATH's.
    // ── EVERY PAGE HAS A COLUMN, AND MAP KEEPS YOU ON IT (2026-09-09, Tom) ──
    // "Switching between doesn't mean you lose content. You just change to the
    // Starmap." Grid → Map used to answer 'releases' for everything except an
    // album, so Artists, Radio, Tour, News and About all threw away what you
    // were looking at and handed you the catalogue.
    //
    // MAP_VIEW_FOR pairs a page with its column. ⚠ ITS INVERSE IS WRITTEN OUT BY
    // HAND in goArtistsFn below, so this is NOT one table read both ways — an
    // earlier version of this comment claimed that and it was never true. Adding
    // a route here does not add its return trip; add both, in one edit. An unlisted route still falls to releases: that is the label's
    // default column, not a guess.
    //
    // ⚠ ACROSS THE HORIZON IS NOT IN THIS TABLE AND MUST NOT BE ADDED. Its pair
    // is written by hand at BOTH ends and neither end is a route lookup:
    // goStarFn sends any AtH page to 'ath-curator', and goArtistsFn sends the
    // curator map back to 'ath-all-releases' — the volumes page, organised by
    // drop. The round trip is volumes → map → volumes, and it holds because
    // both halves are explicit.
    const goStarFn = () => {
      this.muteHoverOpen();
      if (isAth) return this.go('ath-curator', true);
      if (route === 'record' && recIdx != null) return this.openAlbum(recIdx, 'releases');
      // ── THE LANDING OPENS THE WHOLE LABEL (2026-09-09, Tom) ──────────────
      // "If they hit map mode while on a landing page, it should show the full
      // label album cluster in the Starmap." The landing is the label entire —
      // it is not showing a filtered set — so arriving at the map from it clears
      // any facet, constellation or result set still in state and shows every
      // record in the field. This is ahead of the setOpen branch on purpose: a
      // filter left open from an earlier visit is not what the landing means.
      if (route === 'landing') {
        this.setState({ browseSel: null, conAnchor: null, constellation: false, resultCat: null, resultLabel: null, picks: [] });
        return this.go('releases', false);
      }
      // A live filter outranks the route: you are looking at results.
      if (setOpen) return this.go('results', false);
      return this.go(this.MAP_VIEW_FOR[route] || 'releases', false);
    };
    const goArtistsFn = () => {
      this.muteHoverOpen();
      // ── ON ATH, GRID MEANS THE VOLUMES PAGE (2026-09-11, Tom) ──────────────
      // "Anytime all of the catalogue is showing on a Starmap for Across the
      // Horizon, it has to pop back to this… I just want the catalogue always
      // to be organised in the drops, like the volumes page."
      //
      // This returned 'ath-artists' unconditionally, so Grid could not reach
      // the volumes page from the map AT ALL — the one view that carries AtH's
      // actual structure. And a flat A-to-Z of 48 covers is not a lesser view
      // of this label, it is the WRONG view: a drop is one release with three
      // artists chosen by a curator who plays it too, so a grid that splits
      // those three apart and drops the curator has thrown away the only thing
      // the label is organised by. On the other four labels a flat grid loses
      // nothing, which is why this rule is AtH's alone.
      //
      // THE EXCEPTION IS A MIXED SET, and it is not a special case so much as
      // the same rule seen from the other side: a filter or a search returns
      // records from MANY drops, so there is no drop to organise them under.
      // Forcing those into the volumes page would either hide the matches or
      // show whole drops that only partly match. A mixed set gets the flat
      // grid because a flat grid is what a mixed set IS.
      if (isAth) {
        const filtered = !!(st.picks || []).length || st.browseSel != null
          || st.conAnchor != null || view === 'results';
        if (filtered) return this.goRoute('catalogue');
        // The column's own view still wins where it names something specific:
        // an album returns its page, the artists list returns the artists
        // index. Everything else — the catalogue, the whole label, a bare map
        // — is "all of the catalogue", and that is the volumes page.
        const v = starHoverNarrow ? (st.view || 'releases') : view;
        if (v === 'album') return this.openRecord(recIdx != null ? recIdx : (st.lock != null ? st.lock : 0));
        if (v === 'artists') return this.goRoute('ath-artists');
        return this.goRoute('ath-all-releases');
      }
      // Grid is the flat view of WHATEVER the map is currently showing, so it
      // reads the star column's own view rather than always meaning artists:
      // an album in the column returns that album's page, the releases list
      // returns the catalogue grid, the artists list returns the artists
      // index. Off the map it stays inside the current category.
      if (isStar) {
        // The DERIVED view, not st.view: Browse → Artists leaves st.view at
        // 'releases' while the column is showing the artists list, so reading
        // raw state sent Grid to the catalogue. This is the same value the
        // sibling isArtists / isReleases gates read. A transient phone hover
        // is excluded so it can't make Grid mean 'album'.
        const v = starHoverNarrow ? (st.view || 'releases') : view;
        if (v === 'album') return this.openRecord(recIdx != null ? recIdx : (st.lock != null ? st.lock : 0));
        // The inverse of MAP_VIEW_FOR: a column that has a page of its own
        // returns to it, so Map → Grid keeps what you were looking at the same
        // way Grid → Map does (2026-09-09, Tom). Releases and results stay on
        // the catalogue grid — that IS their page.
        if (v === 'artists') return this.goRoute('artists');
        if (v === 'radio' || v === 'tour' || v === 'news' || v === 'about') return this.goRoute(v);
        return this.goRoute('catalogue');
      }
      return (route === 'catalogue' || route === 'record') ? this.goRoute('catalogue') : this.goRoute('artists');
      };

    // THE QUEUE AS SHOWN. One list feeds the chip, the rows, the summary and
    // the "Twelve/Thirteen" label so they cannot disagree. `qover` appends a
    // demo overflow row so I can see how a 13th item wraps — that is a design
    // tool affordance and it is OFF in site mode, where the list is exactly
    // what the host injected (or [], per SITE_EMPTY_WHEN_ABSENT).
    const isSite = !!(this.props.site || (typeof window !== 'undefined' && window.__DC_SITE));
    const qBase = this.data('QUEUE');
    const qPlayed = st.qPlayed || [];
    // The host's queue, then what has been played in this session — deduped on
    // title + artist, so a row the host already carries is not listed twice and
    // the playing mark simply lands on the row that is already there.
    const qMerged = qBase.concat(qPlayed.filter(r => !qBase.some(b => b[0] === r[0] && b[1] === r[1])));
    const qList = (st.qover && !isSite) ? qMerged.concat([['Ridgeline', 'East Forest', 244, true]]) : qMerged;
    // The current artist's social links, from the SOCIALS table (object keyed by
    // artist name → { instagram, site, spotify, apple, bandcamp, youtube }).
    const soc = (this.data('SOCIALS') || {})[this.curArtist()] || {};
    // Resolves across VPS then PROBE_VPS, so a token extractor can address a
    // threshold width by index without the picker ever showing one.
    //
    // ⚠ AN UNKNOWN INDEX THROWS (2026-09-02). It used to fall through to
    // VPS[0], and that silently cost a measurement: an injected probe index
    // resolved to 1180×760 and the column count came back non-monotonic. A
    // fallback that produces a PLAUSIBLE viewport for a wrong index is worse
    // than no fallback, because the result looks like data. The picker cannot
    // generate an out-of-range index — it is driven by VPS.length — so nothing
    // a user does reaches this.
    // ── SITE MODE ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    // `site` = true means this is running as the actual website, in a real
    // browser window, and NOT as a mockup on the design tool's canvas.
    //
    // Added 2026-09-03 because the build was having to patch three literals to
    // get here — the stage's 10px radius, its 80px drop shadow, and the 48px
    // gutter in componentDidMount. They were right that all three are the
    // TOOL's presentation of a fixed-size artboard, and right to be uneasy
    // about deciding that for themselves. It is a mode, so nobody has to.
    //
    // In site mode the real window IS the viewport: VPS and the picker are a
    // set of widths I check the design AT, never a set of widths it runs at.
    const site = !!(this.props.site || (typeof window !== 'undefined' && window.__DC_SITE));
    const VP = this.VPS[st.vp] || this.PROBE_VPS[st.vp - this.VPS.length];
    if (!site && !VP) throw new Error('vp index ' + st.vp + ' is outside VPS (' + this.VPS.length + ') + PROBE_VPS (' + this.PROBE_VPS.length + ')');
    const W = site ? (st.winW || 1180) : VP[0];
    const H = site ? (st.winH || 760) : VP[1];
    const narrow = W < 700;
    // A SHORT phone as well as a narrow one. The ATH cards are the piece that
    // explains the series, so they have to hold all their information without
    // scrolling — and an SE leaves ~483px of card where a 390×844 leaves ~740.
    // Two mobile treatments, not one: `narrow` restructures, `tight` then cuts
    // the remaining vertical spend to fit the short frames.
    const tight = narrow && H < 700;
    // ── A NARROW DESKTOP IS ITS OWN TIER (2026-09-09, Tom) ──────────────────
    // "This 1180 narrower window: is it possible to make all the elements in
    // the header smaller so there's more room? It seems like the choice."
    //
    // It is the choice, and there was no tier to make it in: `narrow` means
    // PHONE (<700), so every desktop width from 700 to 2048 got identical
    // chrome — 32px controls, 40px gutters, a 20px gap — sized for the room a
    // 16-inch window has. At 1180 that is the same furniture in 548 fewer
    // pixels, which is why the header ran out of row.
    //
    // 1300, NOT A NEW NUMBER. The file already branches there in five places,
    // and it is the top of the band 1180 sits in (1100–1300). Choosing it means
    // this tier changes nothing that was not already changing at that width —
    // a new breakpoint would have been a sixth thing to keep in step.
    //
    // WHAT SNUG DOES: shortens the controls (32 → 28), closes the gutters
    // (40 → 26) and the gaps (20 → 13, group 12 → 8), and drops the row itself
    // (72 → 64). Type is untouched — 9px mono is already the floor in this
    // chrome, and shrinking labels to buy room is how a header becomes
    // unreadable rather than smaller. The room comes from spacing and box
    // height, which is where the slack actually was.
    const snug = !narrow && W < 1300;
    // The mode toggles' shell — two-thirds of the header row, shared by View and
    // Audio so a change reaches both. See modeStackStyle for the stack geometry.
    // Full row height now the label is beside it rather than above — the same
    // 32/28 as Browse, so the whole run sits on one line of boxes.
    const modeSeg = {
      display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '2px', padding: '2px',
      height: snug ? '28px' : '32px',
      boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '5px'
      // ⚠ NO BORDER HERE. The shell is declared before the header's ink ladder
      // exists (cHair derives from route, a later local), so the hairline is
      // applied at viewSegStyle / audioSegStyle below — one shell, one ladder,
      // and no second answer to "is this drawn on a photograph".
    };
    // THE TIGHT CUTS — the complete list, corrected 2026-09-01. Four branches
    // REMOVE something; every other tight branch is a number.
    //   athCoreP2Style      · the core card's second paragraph
    //   athCoreKickerStyle  · its italic kicker
    //   jcHeroDekStyle      · the journal lead's standfirst (three lines a
    //                         short phone cannot spare)
    //   athStrands rows     · the BROADCAST strand rows, dropped entirely.
    //     (An earlier version of this list called this "buildDrops rows · the
    //     drop rail's numbered rows" — wrong twice over: buildDrops uses tight
    //     once and it is a padding number, and athDrops has no tight branch at
    //     all. The rows:(tight ? [] : …) construct appears exactly once in this
    //     file and it is athStrands. A wrong name in a corrected list is the
    //     failure this file's blocks exist to catch; caught by the build side.)
    //     ⚠ AND SEE RULING 8 — this cut is now conditional on the rows being
    //     placeholders. With real episodes it becomes 1 row, not 0.
    // An earlier answer of mine said "exactly two", which was wrong: the count
    // came from a search for a literal display:'none', which cannot see a
    // ternary that puts 'none' in its true branch, nor a rows:(tight ? [] : …).
    // Cut all four — none has a shrink value, and inventing one is not the fix.
    // ⚠ A NULL LABEL MUST NOT LEAVE LB UNDEFINED (2026-09-09). Rule 1: "Across
    // the Horizon has no label index and never will — its row carries null where
    // the other four carry 0-3." This read LABELS[st.label] bare, so st.label
    // null gave LB undefined and the next LB[0] threw — the whole page blank.
    // Found by driving an ATH state with label null, which is exactly the state
    // rule 1 says exists.
    //
    // In practice arrival() leaves label at 0 for an ATH visitor and the two
    // readers that matter (siteName, colOwner) both check athSite first, which
    // is why this has never fired. That makes it precisely the "latent instance
    // of the bug that blocked a launch" rule 1 warns about, and it costs one
    // fallback to close.
    //
    // The fallback is LABELS[0] for LOOKUP ONLY. It does not answer "whose site
    // is this" — labelKeyOf is still the only reader of identity, and it is
    // declared on the next line for exactly that reason.
    const LB = this.LABELS[st.label] || this.LABELS[0];
    // §1 · WHOSE SITE THIS IS — carried from arrival, never read off the route.
    // Everything below that answers "which brand am I wearing" reads athSite;
    // everything that answers "which page am I drawing" keeps reading `isAth`,
    // which is still the route prefix (declared further down with its own note).
    const labelKey = this.labelKeyOf(st);
    const athSite = labelKey === 'across-the-horizon';
    // The name of the site the visitor is on, hoisted because the header, the
    // sheet's kicker and the stop frames (§4-4/4-5) all have to say the same
    // thing. Three copies of this expression is how they would drift.
    const siteName = (athSite || st.athCat) ? 'Across the Horizon' : LB[0];
    // ── THE SHORT WORDMARK (2026-09-09, Tom) ────────────────────────────────
    // "For Northern Spy on the small screen you can put NSPY all caps. That's
    // our acronym. For NNA Tapes just NNA. Egghunt should fit, and WSR."
    //
    // Better than the previous answer, which was to drop the name entirely: the
    // mark alone is the label's identity to someone who already knows it, and a
    // four-letter acronym is identity to someone who does not. These are the
    // label's OWN short forms, not truncations — NSPY and WSR are what the
    // labels call themselves, which is why they can carry the name at a size
    // where "NORTHERN SPY" cannot.
    //
    // ⚠ AUTHORED, NOT DERIVED. No initialism rule produces NSPY from "Northern
    // Spy" or keeps "Egghunt" whole, and a rule that tried would give NS, NT, E
    // and WS. So this is a lookup keyed by labelKey — the file's one reader of
    // label identity (rule 1) — and a label not in it falls back to its full
    // name rather than to a guessed abbreviation. Rule 11: nothing invented. A
    // new label needs its short form added here or it keeps the long one.
    //
    // ATH IS ABSENT ON PURPOSE. Its mark already reads "Across the Horizon", so
    // it has no text at any width — the rule that this whole treatment came
    // from. Clandestine and the group site are unaffected: neither draws this
    // header (clsPage / isGroup).
    const SHORT_NAME = {
      'northern-spy': 'NSPY',
      'nna-tapes': 'NNA',
      'egghunt': 'Egghunt',
      'whited-sepulchre': 'WSR'
    };
    const shortName = SHORT_NAME[labelKey] || siteName;
    // The star map can be showing ATH's catalogue rather than the picked label's.
    // AND — 2026-09-07, Tom — MAP and GRID reached from ATH are ATH's surfaces,
    // so an ATH arrival names ATH in the column too. This was the visible half
    // of §1: the heading read NORTHERN SPY · 42 OF 42 over ATH's own 42 records.
    const colOwner = (st.athCat || athSite) ? 'Across the Horizon' : LB[0];
    const view = st.hoverStar != null && st.view !== 'album' ? 'album' : st.view;
    // ── THE LANDING DECK IN THE MAP'S COLUMN (2026-09-09, Tom) ──────────────
    // "I just want the mobile view of the landing page in the sidebar of the
    // Starmap version of Across the Horizon."
    //
    // Every ATH card's geometry is computed from three locals — narrow, tight
    // and W — so the same card code lays out for a phone only when the STAGE is
    // phone-width. The column is 380 inside an 1180 stage, so the cards read
    // aNarrow / aTight / aW instead: the COLUMN's answers while the column is
    // showing the deck, the viewport's everywhere else. phoneMap(st, W, H) has
    // always taken its width this way; this is the same move for the other six
    // cards.
    //
    // ⚠ The landing page is untouched by construction: athCol can only be true
    // on the star route, where isAth is false and no card markup renders. Grid
    // mode reads narrow / tight / W exactly as it did.
    // ⚠ ROUTE AS WELL AS VIEW, and this cost a broken landing page. A VIEW
    // OUTLIVES THE ROUTE THAT SET IT: leaving the map for ATH's own landing
    // keeps 'ath-curator' in st.view, so with view alone as the test the landing
    // computed every card at 380 inside an 1180 stage and their contents spilled
    // out of the panels. The column exists only on the star route — layout
    // belongs to the route (rule 1), and this is a layout question.
    // The chrome's z-index while the Guide's orientation is up: above the wash
    // (30), below the card (32). One local read by every headerStyle branch —
    // there are five, and lifting one is how this fault survived its first fix.
    const guideLift = st.guideIntro ? 31 : 6;
    const athCol = view === 'ath-curator' && st.route === 'star';

    const aW = athCol ? 380 : W;
    const aNarrow = athCol ? true : narrow;
    const aTight = athCol ? false : tight;
    // ── PHONE CHROME, SAFARI ─────────────────────────────────────────────
    // Built for the browser, not the installed app. Safari's viewport already
    // starts BELOW the browser UI, so there is no status bar to inset for and
    // the old 48px reserve was 34px given away on every phone screen. The app
    // build needs env(safe-area-inset-top) instead of a flat number anyway.
    // Declared here because the header, both panes, the sheets, the brackets
    // and the star field all measure from the same edge.
    const PH_TOP = 14;                    // frame edge to the bar
    const PH_BAR = 44;                    // the bar itself (14c)
    // ── PHONE TAP TARGETS · TWO SIZES, AND WHY (2026-09-07, r48) ────────────
    // Twelve controls sat at 32px on a phone — 4px short of the 44 minimum, and
    // they were short because they carry no `narrow` branch at all: the desktop
    // value simply rendered at 390. They now branch to 44.
    //
    // BAR-RESIDENT CONTROLS STAY AT 40, and that is not a compromise. PH_BAR is
    // 44, so a 44px control inside it would touch both edges with no room for
    // the border; 40 is the largest that fits the bar the design already has.
    // The hamburger (44, its own row above the bar), the cart and the theme
    // switch (40, in the bar) were already right and are untouched.
    //
    // So: 44 for anything in the page body, 40 for anything inside the bar. A
    // control that moves from one to the other moves value with it. Anything
    // drawn smaller than a finger — a map dot, a strand node — keeps its drawn
    // size and gets a transparent 44px pad instead; that pattern is unchanged.
    //
    // ⚠ THE COUNT WAS 12 AND THE COUNT WAS WRONG. The handover said "12 of 12
    // controls"; the real class is 25, and the twelve were the ones whose styles
    // happen to be renderVals objects. Reading the source found those and missed
    // every control styled by a literal in the markup — the transport's ⋮ among
    // them. Measuring the rendered frame found all of them, so THAT is the check
    // this rule is verified by from now on: at 390, walk every element whose
    // computed cursor is pointer, take its bounding box, and list anything under
    // 40 on either axis. It is the only pass that cannot be fooled by where a
    // style is written. The nine remaining were: the catalogue card's ⋮ (22×22,
    // the smallest control in the frame), ⤢ Open in both its dresses, Tickets ↗
    // on three separate rails, Get the music ⌄, the artist page's three action
    // chips, and the two full-width contact rows.
    //
    // WHAT IS DELIBERATELY LEFT SHORT: inline text links — the ATH release's
    // "Artist page →" and "All releases →", and Clandestine's @handle. They are
    // text set in a line, not boxes, and padding one to 44 either breaks the
    // line it sits in or draws a button where the design wants a link. The
    // minimum is for controls; a link in a run of text is the documented
    // exception. Anything with a border, a fill or a glyph is a control.
    //
    // AND THE RULE IS PHONE-ONLY, WHICH COST ONE ROUND TO GET RIGHT. Seven of
    // these controls were styled by literals in the markup, where `narrow` is
    // not in scope, so the first pass raised them to 44 unconditionally — which
    // moved seven DESKTOP boxes by 7-10px and left this block describing a rule
    // the code no longer implemented. They are style keys now (the npDotsStyle
    // pattern), branched like the rest, desktop values restored to r47's. If a
    // control needs a breakpoint it needs a style key: a literal in the markup
    // cannot have one, and writing the phone value into it changes both.
    const PH_PANE = PH_TOP + PH_BAR + 12; // 70 · flat pages
    const PH_FIELD = PH_TOP + PH_BAR + 18; // 76 · the star field and its brackets
    const PH_SHEET = PH_FIELD + 18;       // 94 · menu and browse sheets
    const PH_PEEK = 230;                  // drawer left showing at rest
    const PH_GAP = 18;                    // air between the bottom arms and the drawer
    // SQUARE WHEN IT FITS, a horizontal rectangle when it does not — and always
    // with PH_GAP of margin under the bottom arms, so the viewfinder never sits
    // hard against the drawer edge. The screen sets the ceiling; the drawer
    // state sets what is left.
    const PH_FIELD_H = Math.max(200, Math.min(W - 28, H - PH_FIELD - PH_PEEK - PH_GAP));
    // 78 is the field's top, starTailPx its bottom — the same two numbers
    // fieldStyle positions the box with, so the dots fill exactly what the
    // brackets frame. It was a frozen 168 (78 + 90).
    // ── THE VIEWFINDER'S MARGIN (2026-09-10) ──────────────────────────────
    // One number on all four sides of the star field and its brackets: 24 off
    // the stage, 24 off the drawer, and 24 below the 72px header — which is
    // where MG_TOP's 96 comes from, and why the top read as "less margin" than
    // the rest for as long as it was a literal 78.
    //
    // ⚠ DECLARED HERE, above fieldH, because fieldH is its first reader. Put
    // it down beside `gutter` (where it conceptually belongs) and every render
    // throws "Cannot access MG_TOP before initialization" — the same
    // declaration-order trap `gutter` itself carries a note about.
    //
    // ⚠ NOT `gutter` (drawerW + 16). That is the TRANSPORT's clearance, a bar
    // tucking beside the drawer. This is a frame's margin.
    const MG = 24;
    const MG_TOP = 72 + MG;
    const fieldH = narrow ? PH_FIELD_H : H - MG_TOP - this.starTailPx(st);
    const stars = this.field(fieldH);
    const activeStar = st.lock != null && st.view === 'album'
      ? stars.find(s => s.rec === st.lock)
      : (st.hoverStar != null ? stars.find(s => s.id === st.hoverStar) : (st.hoverRow != null ? stars.find(s => s.rec === st.hoverRow) : null));

    // ── THE DRAWER STEPS WITH THE SCREEN (2026-09-01) ─────────────────────
    // It was a flat 380 from 1180 to 2048, so at 2048 the field gained 868px and
    // the album panel gained nothing — the cover sat at the same size on a
    // screen with half again as much room, which is the opposite of what the
    // extra width is for. The drawer is where a record is actually looked at,
    // so it takes a share of the gain: 380 at 1180, 440 at 1728, 500 at 2048.
    //
    // Everything else derives from this one number — the field gutter, the ⟩
    // tab, the four brackets and the queue popover's right edge all read
    // drawerW — so the step propagates and nothing needs a second value.
    // 1180 is deliberately untouched: 380 of 1180 is already a third of the
    // frame, and the field cannot afford more there.
    const D16 = this.props.drawer16 == null ? 440 : this.props.drawer16;
    const D20 = this.props.drawer20 == null ? 500 : this.props.drawer20;
    const drawerW = narrow ? Math.min(340, W - 30) : (W >= 2000 ? D20 : W >= 1700 ? D16 : 380);
    // AND SO DOES THE COVER. Widening the drawer alone spent every new pixel on
    // the title column and left the artwork at 50px on a 20" screen — the exact
    // complaint the step was meant to answer, moved one level in. The release
    // row's cover square steps with it: 50 / 60 / 72. The row's grid reads this
    // number for its first column, so the cover and the text share the gain
    // instead of the text taking it all.
    const C16 = this.props.cover16 == null ? 60 : this.props.cover16;
    const C20 = this.props.cover20 == null ? 72 : this.props.cover20;
    const coverPx = narrow ? 50 : (W >= 2000 ? C20 : W >= 1700 ? C16 : 50);
    const open = st.open;
    const route = st.route;
    const isStar = route === 'star';
    // ── Clandestine palette ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
    // Its own paper, not the site theme's: warm grey #E7E6E0 with near-black
    // ink, flipping to ink-on-paper in dark mode. Each sub-brand tints the
    // paper a few percent toward its own hue so the four are distinguishable
    // without a second palette.
    const clsPage = this.CLS_PAGES[route] || null;
    // ── ONE ANCHOR FOR EVERY HEADER PANEL (2026-09-06, Tom) ─────────────────
    // "These two mouseover menus are in different places. They shouldn't be."
    // Our services and Explore were positioned by two separate style objects,
    // each declaring its own top / right / z-index — two declarations of one
    // relationship, which is this file's most-repeated fault. They had drifted,
    // so the panels hung at different depths under the same bar and moving
    // between two adjacent menus felt like two unrelated pieces of UI.
    //
    // Both spread this now. Changing the drop changes both, and neither can be
    // adjusted alone. top: 100% is the button's own bottom edge, so the pair is
    // flush to the bar on every one of Clandestine's headers; the 10px is the
    // only gap, and it is the same 10px for both.
    const CLS_PANEL_ANCHOR = { position: 'absolute', top: '100%', right: 0, zIndex: 80, marginTop: '10px' };
    // ROUTE 24 — the parent company's own landing page. It is a sealed site with
    // its own masthead and footer, so like the Clandestine pages it suppresses
    // the label chrome entirely and owns the whole frame. The markup is NOT
    // duplicated here: it is imported from "Stellar Atlas Group.dc.html", which
    // stays the single place that page is edited.
    const isGroup = route === 'group';
    const clsDark = st.theme === 'dark';
    const clsBasePaper = '#E7E6E0', clsBaseInk = '#0C100F';
    const clsTint = clsPage ? clsPage.tint : null;
    const clsPaper = clsDark
      ? (clsTint ? clsTint.ink : clsBaseInk)
      : (clsTint ? clsTint.paper : clsBasePaper);
    const clsInk = clsDark
      ? (clsTint ? clsTint.paper : clsBasePaper)
      : (clsTint ? clsTint.ink : clsBaseInk);
    const clsMuted = clsDark ? '#8E968F' : '#7C857F';
  // READING TEXT IS NOT METADATA (2026-09-02). clsMuted is the colour of mono
  // band heads, the location strip and tracked uppercase labels — never of
  // sentences. The enquiry form set 38 questions and 9 descriptors in it, at
  // 3.0:1 on the tinted tiles, and only made them legible AFTER you ticked
  // them — so the text you read to decide was the least readable on the page.
  // clsReadInk is the page ink held back a little: ~9:1, quieter than the
  // 15:1 body copy, still reading text.
  const clsReadInk = 'rgba(' + parseInt(clsInk.slice(1,3),16) + ',' + parseInt(clsInk.slice(3,5),16) + ',' + parseInt(clsInk.slice(5,7),16) + ',0.78)';
    const clsRule = clsDark ? 'rgba(231,230,224,0.18)' : 'rgba(12,16,15,0.16)';
    const clsGold = '#E8B04A';
    // WHOSE ATH artist page this is. `this.curArtist()` may hold a label artist left
    // behind by the previous route, and a label artist has no drop — so resolve
    // to a real roster name rather than rendering the template empty.
    // One resolver, shared with the nav tuple (2026-09-05).
    const athArtWho = this.athArtistOnPage();
    // ── ONE PHOTO RESOLUTION FOR THE ATH ARTIST PAGE (2026-09-15, engineering) ─
    // Tom: "I don't want to see any placeholder photos anywhere. There's no
    // excuse. We know all the artists, we know all the covers." All 47 ATH
    // artist pages were drawing the hatched NO PHOTO YET plate while the
    // photographs sat in public/ath/curator-map/ and the MOSAIC 386 lines below
    // displayed every one of them.
    //
    // Three faults, and they are one fault wearing three hats — the detail page
    // inverted the mosaic's resolution order and then asked the same question
    // twice, in two places, with two different expressions:
    //   1. `has` tested `known[1]` (the INDEX row) while `src` one line above
    //      already fell back to photoFor(). The right URL was computed and
    //      thrown away on the next line. INDEX ships ZERO rows for ATH today.
    //   2. The caption's style repeated the test in its OWN IIFE, so fixing (1)
    //      alone would have painted "NO PHOTO YET" across 47 real photographs.
    //      Worse than the bug.
    //   3. `ar` fell back to 1, squaring up 34 of the 47 files (0.663–1.778) and
    //      cropping them — under two comments in this very block that say NO
    //      CROP and NEVER CROPPED, NEVER STRETCHED.
    //
    // So it is resolved ONCE here, beside athArtWho, and both keys read it. The
    // shape is the mosaic's, deliberately: (row && row[2]) || imgAR(src) || 1.
    //
    // ⚠ imgAR's fallback is REQUIRED EVEN AFTER INDEX ARRIVES: engineering's
    // dropData.ts emits ar: 0 for an unknown size, and `known[2] ? known[2] : 1`
    // treats 0 as falsy and squares up anyway. `||` handles 0 correctly.
    const athArtIdx = this.data('INDEX').filter(x => x[0] === athArtWho)[0];
    // An INDEX row can EXIST with an empty photo path, which is not the same as
    // having a photo: falling back only on a missing row emitted url() — a
    // malformed empty request — and rendered a bare grey tile.
    const athArtPhoto = (athArtIdx && athArtIdx[1]) ? athArtIdx[1] : this.photoFor(athArtWho);
    const athArtHasPhoto = !!athArtPhoto;
    const athArtPhotoAR = (athArtIdx && athArtIdx[2]) || this.imgAR(athArtPhoto) || 1;
    // The gutter reserves room for the SIDE drawer. On a phone the drawer is a
    // bottom sheet, so nothing horizontal is reserved — otherwise the header,
    // field and popovers all get squeezed to zero width.
    // ══ THE GUTTER IS THE ONE RULE FOR EVERYTHING THAT SHARES THE FLOOR WITH THE
    // DRAWER (2026-09-03, written out because the site was getting it wrong):
    //
    //   drawer OPEN  (star view, desktop)  → gutter = drawerW + 16
    //   drawer CLOSED, or any other route  → gutter = 24   (phone: 14)
    //
    // The transport bar's `right` IS this gutter, nothing else — so when the
    // drawer closes the bar must widen to the full stage minus 24px on the same
    // render (eased over EASE's 280ms alongside the drawer's slide), and when
    // it opens the bar must shrink to clear the drawer. There is no second
    // rule and no cached width: the bar reads `open` through this one constant.
    // Measured in the drop at 1180 (layout px): open → bar right 396, width
    // 760; closed → right 24, width 1132, settled by 300ms.
    //
    // If the site's bar keeps the open-width after closing, it has copied the
    // NUMBER (396) rather than the RULE, or it reads a stale `open`. Both are
    // build faults; the acceptance test is in `STARMAP-DRAWER-TRANSPORT.md`.
    const gutter = (!narrow && isStar && open) ? (drawerW + 16) + 'px' : (narrow ? '14px' : '24px');
    // ⚠ DECLARED HERE, NOT BESIDE SHORT_NAME, because it reads drawerW, isStar,
    // `open` and narrow — all of which are declared below siteName. An earlier
    // pass put it up there and every render threw "Cannot access isStar before
    // initialization". The names it needs are the reason it lives after gutter.
    // ── THE ROW GIVES WAY IN A FIXED ORDER, PER LABEL (2026-09-09, Tom) ─────
    // "I'd rather have the full words. If needed, ONLY if needed, remove View
    // and Audio. It could only need it for Northern Spy — Whited Sepulchre might
    // be the same length. Egghunt and NNA Tapes are smaller, they fit."
    //
    // ⚠ THIS REVERSES MY EARLIER RULING and Tom's supersedes it. I had decided
    // the name gives way first, because the name is not a control. He is
    // overriding that: the name is what the visitor came for, the mode labels are
    // a convenience, and "few people have a screen that bad" — so the labels go
    // first and the name is protected for as long as it fits.
    //
    // THREE STEPS, AND WHICH ONE APPLIES DEPENDS ON THE LABEL, not on a width
    // number. That is the whole point of his note: "Egghunt definitely does not"
    // need the sacrifice, "Northern Spy is the longest one". A blanket breakpoint
    // would strip Egghunt's labels to solve Northern Spy's problem.
    //
    //   1 · full name + VIEW / AUDIO labels          — whenever both fit
    //   2 · full name, labels dropped                — when only that fits
    //   3 · short form (NSPY / WSR …) + labels back   — when the full name cannot
    //   4 · below 700 the whole thing is mobile chrome (14c), unchanged
    //
    // Step 3 restores the labels deliberately: a four-letter acronym costs ~50px
    // where the full name costs ~180, so the room the name gave up pays for them
    // twice over. Dropping both at once would be sacrificing something for
    // nothing.
    //
    // ⚠ THE WIDTHS ARE ESTIMATED, NOT MEASURED, and that is a deliberate limit.
    // renderVals cannot measure the row it is about to draw — the only honest
    // measurement happens after paint, and driving layout from it means a second
    // render pass and a loop risk this file has been bitten by (see fitPanels).
    // So: a per-character estimate for the name, and two measured constants for
    // the chrome, both taken from the real 1180 layout with the drawer open
    // (controls 205→784 with labels, ~68px less without). They are estimates of
    // a fixed thing, so they are stable — but a control added to this row means
    // re-measuring CHROME_LABELLED, and there is nothing that will remind you.
    // ⚠ THE CONSTANTS BELOW ARE MEASURED, and the ones they replace were not.
    // The first pass guessed 511 / 579 / 72 and a flat 11.6px per character; all
    // four were low, together by 61px, so fitsBare granted tier 2 to a row that
    // needed 61px more than budgeted and Explore ran 15px past its own
    // container. These are read off the real 1180 layout with the drawer open:
    // Browse + both toggles 319, Explore 79, theme and cart and the gaps between
    // them the remainder.
    //
    // HEADROOM EXISTS BECAUSE A TIER GRANTED WITH 1px TO SPARE IS NOT GRANTED.
    // Before it, the ladder would hand out a tier that fitted exactly and any
    // rounding, any font substitution, any future 2px of padding put Explore
    // under the drawer again. 20px is the margin that makes the decision hold.
    const NAME_FONT = '600 18px Archivo, Helvetica, sans-serif';
    const NAME_TRACK = 0.36;             // 0.02em at 18px, which measureText omits
    const CHROME_LABELLED = 640;
    const CHROME_BARE = 572;
    const MARK_W = 62;
    const HEADROOM = 20;
    // The row's real content box: the header is left 16 (snug) or 24, and right
    // `gutter` — so this is not an estimate at all, it is the same two numbers
    // headerStyle itself uses.
    const rowRoom = W - (snug ? 16 : 24) - ((isStar && open && !narrow) ? (drawerW + 16) : 24);
    const nameBudget = rowRoom - MARK_W - HEADROOM;
    // Real glyph advances, not a per-character rate — see _textW for why no
    // single rate can be right for both "NNA TAPES" and "Whited Sepulchre".
    // ATH carries no text at any width, so 0 puts it in tier 1 by arithmetic
    // rather than by a branch.
    const nameW = athSite ? 0 : this._textW(String(siteName).toUpperCase(), NAME_FONT, NAME_TRACK);
    const fitsWithLabels = nameW <= nameBudget - CHROME_LABELLED;
    const fitsBare = nameW <= nameBudget - CHROME_BARE;
    // ATH never carries text (its mark reads the name), so it is always step 1 —
    // nameW 0 makes that fall out of the arithmetic rather than needing a branch.
    const modeLabelsShown = fitsWithLabels || !fitsBare;
    const wordmarkShort = !fitsBare;
    const cols = narrow ? 2 : (W >= 1700 ? 6 : 5);
    // ── THE HERO SET IS THE REAL HEROES (2026-09-06, r45 review) ────────────
    // ⚠ Filtered HERE rather than at each affordance, because this turn's
    // `pending` text gate turned a latent inconsistency into a dead end: with
    // heroKicker/Title/Artist returning '' AND heroScrollCueStyle already
    // hiding the pager on `hero.pending`, tapping through to a pending hero
    // gave a full-bleed photograph with no words, no dots and no tap pads. The
    // wheel still worked, which on a phone means nothing — exactly the state
    // the tappable pager was built in r43 to fix.
    //
    // The real fault is older and worth naming: **a pager describes the SET,
    // not the current item**, so gating it on `hero.pending` was always wrong.
    // And this turn's heroOthers change (exclude pending rows) left the two
    // navigation affordances disagreeing about how many heroes exist.
    //
    // One source settles both: a pending row is not a destination, so it is
    // not in the set. `heroAll.length < 2` then correctly collapses the pager
    // when there is only one real hero, and heroOthers' filter becomes a
    // no-op that agrees by construction.
    //
    // The single pending row survives only when NOTHING is real — that is the
    // honest first frame (r32), and it must still render or `hero` is
    // undefined and the whole surface throws.
    const heroAll = (() => {
      const all = this.heroSet(st.label);
      const real = all.filter(h => h && !h.pending);
      return real.length ? real : all.slice(0, 1);
    })();
    const heroIdx = Math.min(heroAll.length - 1, st.hero || 0);
    const hero = heroAll[heroIdx];
    // Landing chrome floats over a photograph, so it is light-on-dark in BOTH
    // themes (12a's note): the theme only shows up when a panel opens.
    // ⚠ THE ROUTE FACT, NOT THE BRAND FACT (2026-09-07, §1). This decides which
    // PAGE is being drawn — the ATH deck, its panels, its fit passes, its
    // transport rule — and it must stay keyed on the route, because those
    // layouts belong to ATH's own pages and not to the group's map and grid.
    // For "whose site is this", read athSite above. The two were one flag, and
    // that is exactly how the chrome fell to Northern Spy on `star`.
    const isAth = route.indexOf('ath-') === 0;
    // The full stacked lockup belongs to the ATH landing alone; every other ATH
    // page wears the standard bar — icon plus wordmark, same as the other labels.
    // THE LOCKUP FOLLOWS THE LANDING, AND THE LANDING MOVED (2026-09-04). This
    // read route === 'ath-home', the deleted page, so the full stacked lockup
    // stopped appearing anywhere. ATH's landing is the deck's FIRST card — the
    // idea — so that is where the big lockup belongs; every other ATH page,
    // including the deck's later cards, wears the standard bar.
    // ── ATH WEARS THE TEXT LOGO, ALWAYS (2026-09-04, Tom) ──────────────────
    // ⚠ THIS WAS MY REGRESSION AND IT WAS INTRODUCED TWICE.
    // The decision — never the full lockup in the header, the text logo in
    // every state, no transformation on scroll — was taken and held. Then r9
    // deleted the ath-home route and I "followed the landing" by re-pointing
    // athBig at the deck's first card, which put the full mark back on the ATH
    // landing and made it shrink on scroll. Re-pointing a flag at a new route
    // is not the same as honouring why the flag was retired: the header is
    // fixed chrome, and chrome that changes as you scroll asks to be watched
    // instead of used.
    //
    // So it is FALSE, permanently, rather than pointed somewhere new. Every
    // reader below — lockMark, lockWrapStyle, chromeHeaderBar, the header
    // paddings, the phone plate — keeps its second state, which is the one
    // that was decided. The const survives (nine call sites read it) but it
    // can no longer become true, and this comment is here so a future route
    // change cannot repeat the same mistake a third time.
    const athBig = false;
    const onPhoto = route === 'landing';
    const cInk = onPhoto ? '#f3f1ea' : 'var(--ink)';
    const cInk70 = onPhoto ? 'rgba(243,241,234,0.78)' : 'var(--ink70)';
    const cInk55 = onPhoto ? 'rgba(243,241,234,0.6)' : 'var(--ink55)';
    const cHair = onPhoto ? 'rgba(243,241,234,0.2)' : 'var(--hair20)';
    // The View / Audio toggles' three colours, as one object — the unheld word,
    // the held word and its tint. On the landing all three come off the photo
    // ladder; everywhere else they are the theme's (2026-09-09, Tom).
    const modeLadder = {
      ink: cInk70,
      gold: onPhoto ? '#DBBF3D' : 'var(--gold)',
      tint: onPhoto ? 'rgba(219,191,61,0.18)' : 'var(--goldtint)'
    };
    // Follow destinations per label — Instagram, Spotify, Bandcamp, in the order
    // the panel lists them. Kept as its own table rather than extra columns on
    // LABELS, because LABELS is the MARK table and every consumer of it reads
    // positionally. Spotify is a search URL where we have no artist/label id;
    // swap in the real profile URL as each is confirmed.
    const FOLLOW = [
      ['https://instagram.com/northernspyrecords', 'https://open.spotify.com/search/Northern%20Spy%20Records', 'https://northernspyrecords.bandcamp.com'],
      ['https://instagram.com/nnatapes', 'https://open.spotify.com/search/NNA%20Tapes', 'https://nnatapes.bandcamp.com'],
      ['https://instagram.com/egghuntrecords', 'https://open.spotify.com/search/Egghunt%20Records', 'https://egghuntrecords.bandcamp.com'],
      ['https://instagram.com/whitedsepulchrerecords', 'https://open.spotify.com/search/Whited%20Sepulchre%20Records', 'https://whitedsepulchrerecords.bandcamp.com']
    ][st.label] || ['', '', ''];

    const EASE = 'transform 280ms cubic-bezier(.4,0,.2,1), right 280ms cubic-bezier(.4,0,.2,1), opacity 200ms';

    // The gold nav item is part of the recreated header (3a, 7d, 7l, 7m, 7o each
    // gold their own): on a flat page the route decides, not the star column's view.
    const ROUTE_NAV = {
      artists: 'artists', artist: 'artists', tour: 'tour', news: 'news', about: 'about', radio: 'radio', catalogue: 'releases',
      // 15b draws Releases gold on the ATH home, so home lights Releases.
      // 'ath-all-releases' ADDED 2026-09-09 (code audit part 5): the menu item is
      // keyed 'ath-all-releases' and the table had no such row, so Volumes stayed
      // ink on its own page while its sibling Artists lit correctly.
      'ath-all-releases': 'ath-all-releases',
      'ath-home': 'ath-releases', 'ath-releases': 'ath-releases', 'ath-artists': 'ath-artists',
      'ath-map': 'ath-map',
      'ath-artist': 'ath-artists', 'ath-journal': 'ath-journal', 'ath-broadcast': 'ath-broadcast',
      'ath-about': 'ath-about'
    };
    // The label nav, computed ABOVE the object literal because navItems and
    // menuItems both read it. NNA Tapes has no Radio: the item was removed
    // 2026-08-19 because it opened Northern Spy's Lot Radio show. ATH merges
    // Radio and Podcast into Broadcast and carries its own list entirely.
    // RULING 3 — Tom's Slack, 2026-08-18: "artists - catalog, on tour - radio -
    // news - about follow us and explore". Artists leads, Radio sits before
    // News, and there is a Follow Us row. RADIO IS NORTHERN SPY ONLY
    // (st.label === 0) — this used to read "every label except NNA", which gave
    // Egghunt and Whited Sepulchre a Radio row they do not have.
    const LABEL_MENU = [['Artists', 'artists'], ['Releases', 'releases'], ['Tour', 'tour']]
      // §1 · KEYED ON THE CARRIED LABEL, NOT THE INDEX (2026-09-07, Tom: "the
      // mark and the hamburger menu reflect that top menu every time until
      // somebody goes to Explore and switches"). Radio is Northern Spy's alone,
      // and it has to still be Northern Spy's alone on the star map and the
      // grid — so the gate reads the same key the mark reads. One authority for
      // "whose site is this", used by the mark and the menu alike; two would
      // eventually disagree, which is the whole of §1.
      // ── RADIO IS OFF THE MENU (2026-09-09, Tom) ────────────────────────────
      // "Let's remove Radio from Northern Spy altogether. Grid or map, it's just
      // that there's not a lot there. It's old."
      //
      // It was Northern Spy's alone and reached from both the desktop nav and
      // the hamburger, in grid and in map. Removing the row removes all four,
      // because LABEL_MENU is the one list both read — which is also why this is
      // the only edit needed.
      //
      // ⚠ THE ROUTE AND THE PAGE STAY. 'radio' is still in ROUTES, still a
      // picker entry, and isRadioPage still draws — so a live /radio URL and an
      // installed drop that links to one keep working, and an inbound
      // __DC_GO({route:'radio'}) still lands. What goes is the invitation, which
      // is what "not a lot there" is about. Deleting the surface would be a
      // second, larger decision and would break an address that exists.
      //
      // The drawer view goes with the menu item in practice — nothing routes to
      // view 'radio' any more — but isRadio and its column are left defined for
      // the same reason: reachable by URL, and the TITLES row it needs is still
      // there. Tell me to cut the page and I will cut all of it.
      .concat([['News', 'news'], ['About', 'about']]);
    // FOLLOW US IS NOT IN THIS LIST. It sits last in the nav row but it is a
    // HOVER PANEL, not a route — it never navigated anywhere, which is the bug
    // Tom caught. Rendered as followBtnStyle beside the routed items so it reads
    // as one nav row, opened on hover like Explore. See followRows below.

    // FIVE across on every desktop width — the covers simply get bigger on a
    // 16" or 20" screen rather than more of them fitting. Two on a phone.
    // Computed here rather than inside catGridStyle because it is read as a
    // string there.
    const catCols = narrow ? 2 : 5;

    // The ATH roster in RELEASE order, newest first: Volume Two's drop 8 back to
    // Volume One's drop 1, and inside each drop the curator then the two they
    // invited. Declared here because the count line and the mosaic both read it.
    const athRoster = (() => {
      const out = [];
      const curators = {};
      for (let vi = this.data('VOLS').length - 1; vi >= 0; vi--) {
        // Guarded for the same reason as `sel` above: a VOLS row without a
        // `drops` array would throw here and take the whole render with it.
        const drops = (this.data('VOLS')[vi] || {}).drops || [];
        for (let di = drops.length - 1; di >= 0; di--) {
          const d = drops[di];
          curators[d[1]] = 1;
          out.push(d[1]);
          (d[3] || []).forEach(g => out.push(g));
        }
      }
      // One tile per name: Marisa Anderson curates Volume One drop 2 and is also
      // invited into drop 4, and the index is a list of artists, not placements.
      // Curating anywhere makes her a curator here.
      return out.filter((n, i) => out.indexOf(n) === i).map(n => ({ name: n, curator: !!curators[n] }));
    })();

    // Frame 2a's 40px square social control. Declared here because six
    // properties share it.
    const socChip = (has) => has ? {
      display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center',
      width: '40px', height: '40px', flexShrink: 0, boxSizing: 'border-box',
      borderRadius: '5px', border: '1px solid var(--hair20)',
      color: 'var(--ink70)', cursor: 'pointer'
    } : { display: 'none' };

    // The journal's filtered run. Computed HERE because jrnCount, jrnHasLead,
    // the lead block and the grid all read it — the handover's rule about a
    // value two properties share never living inside one of them.
    const jrnFilter = st.jrnFilter || 'All';
    const jrnList = jrnFilter === 'All'
      ? this.data('JOURNAL').slice()
      : this.data('JOURNAL').filter(x => x[1] === jrnFilter);
    const jrnLead = jrnList.length ? jrnList[0] : null;
    const jrnRest = jrnList.slice(1);

    const menu = (v) => {
      const on = isStar
        ? (st.view === v || (st.view === 'album' && st.prev === v))
        : ROUTE_NAV[route] === v || (route === 'landing' && v === 'releases');
      const photo = route === 'landing';
      return {
        padding: photo ? '12px 0' : '14px 0', borderRadius: '4px', cursor: 'pointer', whiteSpace: 'nowrap',
        color: on ? (photo ? '#DBBF3D' : 'var(--gold)') : (photo ? 'rgba(243,241,234,0.78)' : 'var(--ink70)'),
        textShadow: photo ? '0 1px 8px rgba(0,0,0,0.65)' : 'none'
      };
    };

    const albumRec = st.hoverStar != null
      ? (stars.find(s => s.id === st.hoverStar) || {})
      : (st.lock != null ? { rec: st.lock } : {});
    const recIdx = st.route === 'record'
      ? (st.recIdx != null ? st.recIdx : (st.lock != null ? st.lock : 0))
      : albumRec.rec;
    const pair = recIdx != null && this.data('PAIRS')[recIdx] ? this.data('PAIRS')[recIdx] : null;

    // NO CSS DOTS WHEN THE REAL FIELD IS MOUNTED. The stand-in is a drawing of
    // a sky; with the WebGL field painting, 489 invisible 40px hit-boxes over
    // it would only steal its pointer events. `stars` still carries all 489 —
    // the DATA array grows, the STAND-IN does not.
    // ⚠ siteMode() FIRST, THEN the flag (2026-09-11). stars[0].hosted alone
    // was the weaker guarantee — it depends on the host stamping a flag onto
    // seat 0, so an absent __DC_STARS defeated it silently and the tool's sky
    // painted on the live site. The structural test cannot be defeated by
    // data; the flag stays so the DESIGN TOOL also stops drawing dots when
    // real seats are supplied to it.
    const starVals = (this.siteMode() || (stars[0] && stars[0].hosted)) ? [] : stars.map(s => {
      const isActive = activeStar && s.id === activeStar.id;
      const near = activeStar && Math.hypot(s.x - activeStar.x, s.y - activeStar.y) <= 150;
      const dotSize = isActive ? 14 : s.size;
      return {
        key: s.id,
        box: {
          position: 'absolute', left: 'calc(' + (s.fx * 100).toFixed(3) + '% - 20px)', top: (s.y - 20) + 'px', width: '40px', height: '40px',
          display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center', cursor: 'pointer',
          zIndex: isActive ? 3 : 1,
          animation: s.anim + ' ' + s.dur + 's ease-in-out ' + s.delay + 's infinite',
          animationPlayState: near ? 'paused' : 'running'
        },
        dot: {
          width: dotSize + 'px', height: dotSize + 'px', borderRadius: '50%',
          background: isActive ? 'var(--gold)' : (st.theme === 'light' ? 'rgba(23,25,27,' + (near ? Math.min(0.9, s.alpha + 0.3) : s.alpha) + ')' : 'rgba(243,241,234,' + (near ? Math.min(0.9, s.alpha + 0.28) : s.alpha) + ')'),
          boxShadow: isActive ? '0 0 26px 10px rgba(219,191,61,0.38)' : 'none'
        },
        hasLabel: !!isActive,
        plate: {
          position: 'absolute', top: '38px', left: '50%', transform: 'translateX(-50%)',
          width: 'max-content', maxWidth: '200px', whiteSpace: 'nowrap', textAlign: 'center', pointerEvents: 'none',
          padding: '2px 6px', background: st.theme === 'light' ? 'rgba(255,255,255,0.94)' : 'rgba(6,7,10,0.92)', borderRadius: '3px',
          display: isActive ? 'inline-block' : 'none'
        },
          // ── "CLICK TO LOCK", ON THE STAR ITSELF (2026-09-10, Tom: "teaching
          // people how to lock a star, that's really important") ─────────────
          // A third line on the plate the visitor is ALREADY reading, rather
          // than a floating tip. Three reasons it belongs here: the instruction
          // is about the thing under the cursor, so it should be under the
          // cursor; the plate is already positioned, already themed and already
          // appears exactly when a star is live, so there is no second anchor
          // to drift; and the field has been reported twice for stray overlays
          // (the freeze rings, r47) — this adds no element that can survive a
          // pan.
          //
          // ⚠ IT RETIRES ITSELF. Shown only until the first successful lock,
          // ever, on this browser. After that the plate is two lines again and
          // never says it a third time. Not a timer and not a dismissal: the
          // visitor demonstrating they know is what removes it, which is the
          // rule every tip in this system follows.
          lockHint: 'Click to lock',
          // ⚠ 9px AND FULL OPACITY, and both were wrong on the first pass. I
          // wrote it at 8px/0.8 — 3.44:1 on the plate's near-white ground, and
          // smaller than the title it sits under. Two rules this file already
          // states: 9px mono is the FLOOR in this chrome, and "8px mono at
          // --ink40 is 2.67:1 and is not legible" is a precedent already paid
          // for once on the toggle captions. Full gold at 9px measures 5.06:1
          // here and 7.21:1 in the dark theme, so no branch is needed.
          //
          // It matters more here than for a label: line 2 is a redundant title,
          // but this line is the only instruction telling a first-time visitor
          // how to operate the map.
          lockHintStyle: (isActive && st.starLock == null && !this.xpHas('map.starLocked'))
            ? { display: 'block', marginTop: '3px', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace",
                fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.16em', textTransform: 'uppercase',
                color: 'var(--gold)' }
            : { display: 'none' },
        artist: s.artist, title: s.title,
        aStyle: { display: 'block', fontSize: '13px', fontStyle: s.ph ? 'italic' : 'normal', color: 'var(--gold)' },
        tStyle: { display: 'block', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', fontStyle: s.ph ? 'italic' : 'normal', color: 'var(--gold)', opacity: 0.75 },
        // A CLICK LOCKS THE STAR. Before this, the card followed the pointer, so
        // reaching it meant crossing other stars and arriving with nothing under
        // the cursor. Now: click to hold it, the music keeps playing, hovering
        // anything else is ignored, and the card's own View opens the album.
        // Clicking the held star again lets go; clicking empty space clears it.
        enter: () => {
          if (st.starLock != null) return;
          if (st.view !== 'album' || st.lock == null) this.hoverEnter({ hoverStar: s.id, prev: st.view === 'album' ? st.prev : st.view });
        },
        leave: () => { if (st.starLock == null) this.setState({ hoverStar: null }); },
        click: (e) => {
          e.stopPropagation();
          if (s.rec == null && st.starLock !== s.id) {
            // ANY star can be held. The guard here used to bail on rec == null,
            // and most of the field carries no catalogue row — so clicking almost
            // anywhere did nothing while hover still looked right, because the
            // column falls back to st.lock for its copy.
            // Locking is the map tip's own answer — see starLocked().
            this.starLocked();
            this.setState({ starLock: s.id, hoverStar: s.id, prev: st.view === 'album' ? st.prev : st.view }, () => this.play(s.rec));
            return;
          }
          if (s.rec == null) return;
          // Releasing: desktop also has to hand the column back to the list it
          // came from, since it holds the real lock.
          if (st.starLock === s.id) {
            this.setState(narrow
              ? { starLock: null, hoverStar: null }
              : { starLock: null, hoverStar: null, lock: null, view: st.prev || 'releases' });
            return;
          }
            // ⚠ EVERY PATH THAT LOCKS, NOT JUST THE FIRST. The mark went on the
            // rec == null branch alone, so a star WITH a record — the common
            // case — locked without recording it and the hint kept offering an
            // instruction the visitor had already followed. Three exits here,
            // two of them lock; starLocked() is on both.
            this.starLocked();
          const base = { starLock: s.id, hoverStar: s.id, started: true, prev: st.view === 'album' ? st.prev : st.view };
          // DESKTOP establishes the file's real lock (st.lock + the album view),
          // which is what lights the active-star ring and turns the field note to
          // "Locked · the music keeps playing". On a phone the lock stays on
          // starLock alone, because st.view === 'album' would swap the compact
          // card for the full album panel.
          this.setState(narrow ? base : Object.assign({}, base, { lock: s.rec, view: 'album' }));
        }
      };
    });

    // THE PAINTED LIST. The drawer, the catalogue grid and the count line all
    // read this one array, so they cannot disagree about how many there are.
    // It used to be PAIRS + three [null] placeholders "so 12 of 179 stays
    // honest" — which made the line honest about a lie: three hatched tiles
    // for records that do not exist. Ruling 1 (no placeholder tiles; counts
    // count what is shown) applies here as everywhere. The window is
    // min(CAP, rows) of what is real.
    // ⚠ THE DESIGN DOES NOT FILTER BY LABEL, AND MUST NOT (stated 2026-09-09,
    // Tom: "I'm in Northern Spy's catalogue and I'm seeing all the releases").
    // PAIRS carries no label field, so there is nothing here to filter ON — the
    // catalogue, the drawer and the count line all render exactly the rows they
    // are given. Scoping a label's catalogue is a HOST re-scope: publish that
    // label's PAIRS (and INDEX) when the label changes, which is the contract
    // the nav bridge already states ("the host uses it to re-scope data").
    //
    // If the design filtered instead, the count line and the grid would disagree
    // with whatever the host thinks it published, and a label with a record the
    // table does not tag would lose it silently. One authority: the table.
    const painted = this.data('PAIRS').slice(0, this.paintCap());
    const releases = painted.map((p, i) => {
      const ph = !p[0];
      const active = st.lock === i && st.view === 'album' ? 2 : (st.hoverRow === i || (st.hoverStar != null && recIdx === i)) ? 1 : 0;
      // ── "AM I PLAYING", NOT "AM I FIRST" (2026-09-04, ledger 31) ──────────
      // This read `i === 0`, so the level meter appeared on row one for ever,
      // whatever was playing and whether anything was. The transport already
      // knows: npPlay is the record, and paused is paused.
      const playing = !!st.started && !st.paused && st.npPlay === i;
      return {
        key: 'r' + i,
        box: {
          position: 'relative', display: 'grid', gridTemplateColumns: coverPx + 'px 1fr 1.5fr 24px', alignItems: 'center', gap: '10px',
          padding: '7px 22px', minHeight: Math.max(60, coverPx + 10) + 'px', boxSizing: 'border-box', cursor: 'pointer', overflow: 'hidden',
          borderBottom: '1px solid var(--hair)',
          background: this.rowBg(active), boxShadow: this.rowBar(active),
          fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '12px'
        },
        cover: {
          width: coverPx + 'px', height: coverPx + 'px', borderRadius: '5px', boxSizing: 'border-box',
          border: '1px solid ' + (active ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--hair20)'),
          background: ph || !p[3]
            ? 'repeating-linear-gradient(135deg, var(--tile) 0 6px, transparent 6px 12px)'
            : 'url(' + this.res(p[3]) + ') center/cover no-repeat, #0a0e1a'
        },
        who: ph ? 'artist' : p[0],
        // ── THE ARTIST LEADS · SECOND OF TWO BUILDERS (2026-09-10) ──────────
        // ⚠ THE SAME FIX WAS APPLIED TO THE ATH COLUMN'S ROW (search
        // "THE ARTIST LEADS" — grid '44px 1fr 1.2fr 22px') AND MISSED THIS ONE,
        // so for one draft the file stated one hierarchy in the column and the
        // opposite in this drawer. Two builders draw a release row: the ATH
        // column's and this one (grid coverPx + 'px 1fr 1.5fr 24px'). If a
        // third appears it must take these values too.
        //
        // CLAUDE.md: "Never an album title without its artist"; on the map
        // "Artist on top, title beneath — the byline leads". Side by side the
        // hierarchy has to live in WEIGHT, since there is no above and below.
        whoStyle: { color: ph ? 'var(--ink55)' : 'var(--ink)', fontStyle: ph ? 'italic' : 'normal', overflow: 'hidden', textOverflow: 'ellipsis', whiteSpace: 'nowrap' },
        ttl: ph ? 'from catalogue' : p[1],
        ttlStyle: { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '8px', minWidth: 0, paddingRight: '18px', color: active === 2 ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--ink70)', fontStyle: ph ? 'italic' : 'normal' },
        ttlTextStyle: { minWidth: 0, overflow: 'hidden', textOverflow: 'ellipsis', whiteSpace: 'nowrap' },
        // The meter shows whenever something is sounding, hover included, and
      // sits at its floor when the host publishes no LEVEL (2026-09-06).
      // ── THE ROW METER MARKS THE PLAYING ROW (2026-09-06, Tom) ──────────────
      // r41 restored these on _sounding(), which is a global "is audio
      // running" — so every row in the list would have shown a meter at once,
      // which says nothing. A meter in a LIST is an indicator of WHICH row is
      // sounding; that is what it was for before r21 took it out.
      //
      // So: this row's record is the one playing, and not paused. Hover
      // previews count, because on the star field and the grid the preview IS
      // the playback — same rule as the transport's glyph (_sounding).
      barsStyle: (() => {
        const t = this._playTuple(st);
        const live = (st.started && !st.paused && st.npPlay === i)
          || (t && t.hoverAudio && t.hoverRec === i);
        return { display: live ? 'flex' : 'none', alignItems: 'flex-end', gap: '1px', height: '12px', flexShrink: 0 };
      })(),
        // ── THE METER HAS SEEN THE AUDIO (2026-09-04, r5 ask 4) ────────────
        // Bars on a canned 900ms CSS loop: it animated whether anything was
        // playing or not, and drew the same shape for silence as for a full
        // mix. LEVEL is the host's live signal — 0..1 band magnitudes on the
        // transport callback. No signal, or paused, and the bars sit QUIET at
        // their floor. Never a fake animation.
        // The drawer row's own four bars, bound to the same LEVEL signal as the
      // transport's (r5 ask 4). barsStyle already hides them unless playing.
      b1: this.vuBar(0), b2: this.vuBar(1), b3: this.vuBar(2), b4: this.vuBar(3),
        enter: () => this.hoverEnter({ hoverRow: i }),
        leave: () => this.setState({ hoverRow: null }),
        // Same ⋮ the catalogue card carries: whatever the grid offers, its star
        // view offers too. Its own handler, and openCtx stops propagation, so
        // the click cannot bubble to the row and open the album instead.
        dotsStyle: ph
          ? { display: 'none' }
          // ⚠ A CONTROL, NOT A DE-EMPHASISED LABEL (2026-09-11) ────────────────
        // Two rules broken at once, and both were already settled in this file.
        //
        // INK: --ink40 resolves to rgba(23,25,27,0.42) in light — 2.67:1 on
        // white, the exact number this file already rules on ("ink40 AT 8px IS
        // 2.67:1 AND THAT IS NOT LEGIBLE") and well under CLAUDE.md's 4.5:1.
        // Nine of these sit on the phone star view. --ink70 is 17.6:1 and is
        // what every other ⋮ placed this session uses; gold marks the active
        // row, as elsewhere.
        //
        // SIZE: 24 × 30 against the ≥40px contract for anything interactive on
        // a phone — "including bare glyphs, which need a box". Same technique
        // as the 14c queue rows and the sheet's mode sides: the element is
        // 40 × 40 and gives its excess back through negative margins, so the
        // hit box is legal and the row's layout is unchanged. The grid's 24px
        // track still governs the column; only the target overflows it.
        : { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center', width: '40px', height: '40px', margin: '-5px -8px', flexShrink: 0, borderRadius: '4px', fontSize: '15px', color: active ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--ink70)', cursor: 'pointer' },
        dots: this.openCtx('catalogue', i),
        click: () => { if (!ph) this.openAlbum(i, 'releases'); }
      };
    });

    const counts = {};
    this.data('PAIRS').forEach(p => { counts[p[0]] = (counts[p[0]] || 0) + 1; });
    const artistRows = Object.keys(counts).map((name, i) => {
      const active = st.hoverRow === 'a' + i ? 1 : 0;
      return {
        key: 'a' + i,
        box: {
